\sum_{m=1}^M E[\zeta_{D,m}(d)]
=
M s_{D,1}(d)
=
1.
\]
Hence \(s_{D,1}(d)=1/M\). Next square the same identity:
\[
\left(\sum_{m=1}^M \zeta_{D,m}(d)\right)^2
=
\sum_{m=1}^M \zeta_{D,m}(d)^2
+
\sum_{\substack{m,r=1\\m\neq r}}^M \zeta_{D,m}(d)\zeta_{D,r}(d)
=
1.
\]
Take expectations and again use exchangeability:
\[
M s_{D,2}(d)+M(M-1)s_{D,11}(d)=1.
\]
This is the claimed identity. \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 4.8D.} Proposition~4.8C is materially weaker than the deterministic bridge \(w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X)=b_D(\mathbf D(\mathbf X))\). It allows residual survey-weight heterogeneity inside one fixed score cell. The price is exactly the one visible in the main theorem path: the first moment still collapses to \(1/M\) by exchangeability, but the second moment carries the genuine local exact-share factors \(s_{D,2}(d)\) and \(s_{D,11}(d)\) rather than the equal-share value \(1/M^2\).

\textbf{Remark 4.8E} (Why neither optional route is a core sampling-sufficiency assumption). The exact four-scenario theorems in Sections~5--8 are stated on the exact-share scale and do \emph{not} assume that the survey weight is determined by the matching score. Proposition~4.8C is only a sufficient route for the exact-share moment-map closure, and Lemma~4.8B is only a stronger sufficient route for the equal-share collapse in Corollary~4.8A. In particular, Proposition~4.8C does \emph{not} by itself assert a global statement such as
\[
S\perp (Y(0),Y(1),Z,\mathbf X)\mid \mathbf D(\mathbf X).
\]
It records only a local conditional matched-side survey-weight law on shrinking score neighborhoods, which is enough to identify the finite-\(n\) exact-share maps in Definition~4.7. In the retrospective design it is fully compatible with arm-specific inclusion probabilities \(\pi_z(\mathbf X)\) that differ across treatment arms. If one interprets the survey weight literally as an inverse inclusion probability and also imposes the stronger identity
\[
w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X)=b_D(\mathbf D(\mathbf X)),
\]
then that stronger optional route amounts to a score-sufficiency statement for the matched-arm sampling rule. Because that statement is stronger than the core WDSM framework and may fail under informative survey designs with additional design variables, the main theorem path never uses either optional route as a primitive identification assumption. In particular, neither optional route is read as asserting score-sufficiency of the whole sampling mechanism, and neither route is allowed to replace the primitive selected-sample recovery assumptions stated in Section~3.



\subsubsection{4.4.5 Four-cell exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moment
corollaries}\label{four-cell-exact-survey-weighted matching-frequency-moment-corollaries}

\textbf{Corollary 4.9} (Retrospective PATE exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moments).
Fix matched arm \(z\in\{0,1\}\) and matched-arm score space
\(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_z(\mathbf X))\). Define
\[P_s\{Z=z,\ \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}=f_{z,z}^s(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w_{1-z}(\mathbf X)\mathbf 1\{Z=1-z,\ \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1-z,z}^{(1),R}(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w_{1-z}(\mathbf X)^2\mathbf 1\{Z=1-z,\ \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1-z,z}^{(2),R}(d)\,\mathrm d d.\]
Assume Closure Input 4.7A holds on this matched-arm score space with cell-specific
share factors
\[s_{z,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d),\qquad s_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d),\qquad s_{z,11}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d).\]
Then \[\kappa_{z,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{z,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(1),R}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)},\]
\[\kappa_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(2),R}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}
+
s_{z,11}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)
\alpha(M,2)
\left\{\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(1),R}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}\right\}^2.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Apply Theorem 4.8 with
\[w_R^{(D_z)}(\mathbf X)=w_{1-z}(\mathbf X),\qquad w_D^{(D_z)}(\mathbf X)=w_z(\mathbf X),\] and
substitute these two cell-specific weights one place at a time into the
generic definitions of \(f_D^s\), \(f_{R,D}^{(1)}\), and
\(f_{R,D}^{(2)}\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 4.10} (Prospective PATE exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moments). Fix
matched arm \(z\in\{0,1\}\) and matched-arm score space
\(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_z(\mathbf X))\). Define
\[P_s\{Z=z,\ \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}=f_{z,z}^s(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w(\mathbf X)\mathbf 1\{Z=1-z,\ \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1-z,z}^{(1),P}(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w(\mathbf X)^2\mathbf 1\{Z=1-z,\ \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1-z,z}^{(2),P}(d)\,\mathrm d d.\]
Assume Closure Input 4.7A holds on this matched-arm score space with cell-specific
share factors
\[s_{z,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(d),\qquad s_{z,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(d),\qquad s_{z,11}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(d).\]
Then \[\kappa_{z,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{z,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(1),P}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)},\]
\[\kappa_{z,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{z,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(2),P}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}
+
s_{z,11}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)
\alpha(M,2)
\left\{\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(1),P}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}\right\}^2.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Apply Theorem 4.8 with
\[w_R^{(D_z)}(\mathbf X)=w(\mathbf X),\qquad w_D^{(D_z)}(\mathbf X)=w(\mathbf X).\] Then substitute the
common prospective weight into the generic marked focal unit densities.
\(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 4.11} (Retrospective PATT exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moments on
the one-sided route). Fix the one-sided control score space
\[\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_0(\mathbf X)).\] Define
\[P_s\{Z=0,\ \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}=f_{0,0}^s(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w_1(\mathbf X)\mathbf 1\{Z=1,\ \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1,0}^{(1),T}(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w_1(\mathbf X)^2\mathbf 1\{Z=1,\ \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1,0}^{(2),T}(d)\,\mathrm d d.\]
Assume Closure Input 4.7A holds on this matched-arm score space with cell-specific
share factors
\[s_{0,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(d),\qquad s_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(d),\qquad s_{0,11}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(d).\]
Then \[\kappa_{0,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{0,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1,0}^{(1),T}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)},\]
\[\kappa_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1,0}^{(2),T}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}
+
s_{0,11}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(d)
\alpha(M,2)
\left\{\frac{f_{1,0}^{(1),T}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}\right\}^2.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Apply Theorem 4.8 with
\[w_R^{(D_0)}(\mathbf X)=w_1(\mathbf X),\qquad w_D^{(D_0)}(\mathbf X)=w_0(\mathbf X).\] Then substitute
the one-sided retrospective PATT weights into the generic marked
focal unit densities. \(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 4.12} (Prospective PATT exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moments on
the one-sided route). Fix the one-sided control score space
\[\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_0(\mathbf X)).\] Define
\[P_s\{Z=0,\ \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}=f_{0,0}^s(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w(\mathbf X)\mathbf 1\{Z=1,\ \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1,0}^{(1),PT}(d)\,\mathrm d d,\]
\[E_s\bigl[w(\mathbf X)^2\mathbf 1\{Z=1,\ \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\in \mathrm d d\}\bigr]=f_{1,0}^{(2),PT}(d)\,\mathrm d d.\]
Assume Closure Input 4.7A holds on this matched-arm score space with cell-specific
share factors
\[s_{0,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(d),\qquad s_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(d),\qquad s_{0,11}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(d).\]
Then \[\kappa_{0,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{0,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1,0}^{(1),PT}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)},\]
\[\kappa_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(d)
=
s_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(d)
M\frac{f_{1,0}^{(2),PT}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}
+
s_{0,11}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(d)
\alpha(M,2)
\left\{\frac{f_{1,0}^{(1),PT}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}\right\}^2.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Apply Theorem 4.8 with
\[w_R^{(D_0)}(\mathbf X)=w(\mathbf X),\qquad w_D^{(D_0)}(\mathbf X)=w(\mathbf X).\] Then substitute the
one-sided prospective PATT weights into the generic marked focal unit
densities. \(\square\)



\textbf{Remark 4.12A.} Corollaries~4.9--4.12 close the \emph{pointwise}
exact survey-weighted matching-frequency first and second moments on the
matching-score scale. They do not by themselves close the \emph{sample}
quadratic-variation limit that appears later in the centered oracle
central limit theorems. The next results therefore add a publication-style
bridge. It is deliberately split into two parts. First, we record a
optional \emph{sufficient} route under the local equal-share validation device. Second, the four cell-specific oracle sections
retain a fallback closure input for the fully general exact-share
route, so that the note does not overstate what has and has not been
proved.

\subsubsection{4.4.6 Optional equal-share validation route (moved out of the main exact-share theorem path)}
\label{optional-equal-share-validation-route-moved}

The optional equal-share collapse and the resulting residual-stabilization validation route are collected in Appendix~P. They are retained only as an auxiliary closed-path device for readers who want a direct bridge back to the equal-share literature. The four main scenario theorems in Sections~5--8 remain stated on the exact-share scale and do not use this optional route as part of the core exact-share theorem object.

\section{5. Retrospective PATE with exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
imputation}\label{retrospective-pate-with-exact-within-match survey-weight-normalized-imputation}

\subsection{5.1 Exact estimator and exact matched unit
load}\label{exact-estimator-and-exact-survey-weighted matching-frequency}

For each focal unit \(i\) with \(Z_i=0\), let \(\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)\) be the
set of its \(M\) nearest treated matched units on the score space
\[\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_1(\mathbf X)).\] In the known-score treated-arm specialization
used here, \[\Psi_1(\mathbf X)=\mu_1(\mathbf X).\] For each focal unit \(i\) with
\(Z_i=1\), let \(\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\) be the set of its \(M\) nearest
control matched units on the score space \[\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_0(\mathbf X)).\] In the
known-score control-arm specialization used here, \[\Psi_0(\mathbf X)=\mu_0(\mathbf X).\]
Define the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized coefficients
\[\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{w_1(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)} w_1(\mathbf X_k)}\,\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)\},\]
for \(Z_i=0\), and \[\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{w_0(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)} w_0(\mathbf X_k)}\,\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\},\]
for \(Z_i=1\).

Define the exact imputed counterfactuals by
\[\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)
:=
\begin{cases}
Y_i,& Z_i=1,\\[4pt]
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j,& Z_i=0,
\end{cases}\] and \[\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)
:=
\begin{cases}
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j,& Z_i=1,\\[4pt]
Y_i,& Z_i=0.
\end{cases}\] The exact retrospective PATE estimator is
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)}.\]
Define the retrospective PATE denominator average
\[\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}
:=
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i).\]

Now define the \textbf{exact} survey-weighted matching frequencies:
\[K_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=0} w_0(\mathbf X_i)
\frac{w_1(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)}w_1(\mathbf X_k)}\,
\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)\},\] and
\[K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)
\frac{w_0(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)}w_0(\mathbf X_k)}\,
\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\}.\] These are the exact weighted reuse
counts generated by the exact estimator. They are not equal to an
equal-share proxy unless all survey weight of the matched units inside every matched
set are equal.

Define the exact matched unit coefficients
\[\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
 w_1(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j),\] and
\[\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
 w_0(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j).\]

\textbf{Lemma 5.1} (Exact matching-frequency rewrite for retrospective PATE).
The exact retrospective PATE estimator can be written as
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Expand only one part at a time: \[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w_0(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w_0(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Re-index the treated-imputation sum by matched unit \(j\):
\[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w_0(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w_0(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}K_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Re-index the control-imputation sum by matched unit \(j\):
\[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Substitute these two identities back into the numerator
and group observed and reuse pieces arm by arm: \[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\bigl\{w_1(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\bigr\}Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\bigl\{w_0(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\bigr\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Finally divide by
\[\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)=n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}.\] \(\square\)

\subsection{5.2 Exact decomposition}\label{exact-decomposition}

\textbf{Lemma 5.2} (Exact decomposition for retrospective PATE). Define
\[H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\},\]
\[B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)(2Z_i-1)
\left[
\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)
\right],\] and \[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\] Then
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
=
H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the focal unit form of the estimator and
subtract the target: \[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\left[\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right].
\end{aligned}\] Add and subtract \(\tau(\mathbf X_i)\) inside the bracket:
\[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\left[\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right]
+
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\left[\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau(\mathbf X_i)\right].
\end{aligned}\] The first sum is exactly the heterogeneity term
numerator. Now analyze the second sum by treatment status.

If \(Z_i=1\), then \[\begin{aligned}
\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau(\mathbf X_i)
&=
Y_i-\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j-\{\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\}\\
&=
\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\{\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)+\varepsilon_{0,j}\}-\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
+
\varepsilon_{1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] If \(Z_i=0\), then \[\begin{aligned}
\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau(\mathbf X_i)
&=
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j-Y_i-\{\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\}\\
&=
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\{\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)+\varepsilon_{1,j}\}-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\varepsilon_{0,i}-\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
-\left[\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)\right]
+
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}-\varepsilon_{0,i}.
\end{aligned}\] Combine the treated and control cases: \[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\left[\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau(\mathbf X_i)\right]\\
&=
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)(2Z_i-1)
\left[
\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)
\right]\\
&\quad+
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\left[\varepsilon_{1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}\right]
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w_0(\mathbf X_i)\left[\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}-\varepsilon_{0,i}\right].
\end{aligned}\] The first line is exactly the bias numerator. Re-index
the residual sums by matched unit exactly as in Lemma 5.1. That gives
\[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\left[\varepsilon_{1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}\right]
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w_0(\mathbf X_i)\left[\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}-\varepsilon_{0,i}\right]\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] Divide by \(n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}\) to finish.
\(\square\)

\subsection{5.3 Exact conditional residual
variance}\label{exact-conditional-residual-variance}

\textbf{Lemma 5.3} (Exact conditional residual variance for
retrospective PATE). Let \(\mathcal F_n\) be the sigma-field generated
by
\[\{\mathbf X_i,Z_i,\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i),\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i),\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}},\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1),\mathrm{ex}}:1\le i,j\le n\}.\]
Then
\[E\!\left[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]=0,\]
and
\[\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].\]

\textbf{Proof.} Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\), the coefficients are
deterministic. Also, \[E[\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=1]=0,
\qquad
E[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0]=0.\] Therefore \[\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)E(\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)E(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=0.
\end{aligned}\] For the conditional variance, start from the exact
matching-frequency residual form:
\[\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\] Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\), the residuals are
independent across sampled units, so all cross terms vanish. Therefore,
\[\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\Bigm|\mathcal F_n
\right)\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}\] \(\square\)

\subsection{5.4 Exact known-score
asymptotics}\label{exact-known-score-asymptotics}

\textbf{Closure Input 5.4} (Retrospective PATE exact residual
stabilization). The predictable quadratic variation converges in probability to a finite nonzero limit \(V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\). Proposition~5.4A below gives one optional equal-share validation route to this closure condition through Corollary~4.8A and Proposition~4.12C:

\[\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right]
\to_p
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\] Moreover, for some \(\delta>0\),
\[\frac{1}{n^{1+\delta/2}\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^{2+\delta}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\left|\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\right|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{1,j}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=1\bigr]
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left|\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\right|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{0,j}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0\bigr]
\right]
\to_p 0.\]


\textbf{Proposition 5.4A} (Optional equal-share subclass implying Closure Input~5.4). Assume Corollary~4.9, Lemma~4.8B, Corollary~4.8A, and Proposition~4.12C on both matched-arm score spaces \(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X)\) and \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\). Then Closure Input~5.4 holds with
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
&=
\frac{
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=1\}\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X)\kappa_{1,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))\right]
+
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=0\}\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X)\kappa_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\right]
}{\left(E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\right)^2},
\end{aligned}
\]
where
\[
\kappa_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(d)
=
\frac1M\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(2),R}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}
+
\frac{\alpha(M,2)}{M^2}
\left\{\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(1),R}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}\right\}^2,
\qquad z\in\{0,1\}.
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Under Lemma~4.8B and Corollary~4.8A, the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized share collapses to the equal-share value \(1/M\). Corollary~4.9 therefore identifies the score-space second-moment limit by changing only the three exact-share factors:
\[
s_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)=\frac1{M^2},
\qquad
s_{z,11}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(d)=\frac1{M^2},
\]
which gives the displayed function \(\kappa_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(d)\). Proposition~4.12C then transfers that score-space equal-share closure to the sample quadratic-variation average, which is exactly the first display in Closure Input~5.4. The Lyapunov display follows from the same proposition together with the bounded \((2+\delta)\)-moments of the residuals. \(\square\)


\textbf{Lemma 5.5} (Retrospective PATE heterogeneity CLT). Assume
\[E_s\left[\left|w_Z(\mathbf X)\{\tau(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}\right|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty,
\qquad
E_s\left[|w_Z(\mathbf X)|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty\]
for some \(\delta>0\). Then
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}\right),\]
where
\[V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
:=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(w_Z(\mathbf X)\{\tau(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}\right)}{\left(E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Define
\[U_i:=w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\},
\qquad
V_i:=w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i).\]
Then
\[H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=\frac{\bar U_n}{\bar V_n},
\qquad
\bar U_n:=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n U_i,
\qquad
\bar V_n:=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n V_i.\]
Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.2, and 2.4, the selected sample is i.i.d., \(E_s[U_i]=0\), and \(E_s[V_i]=E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\in(0,\infty)\). The displayed moment condition implies the bivariate central limit theorem,
\[\sqrt n\binom{\bar U_n}{\bar V_n-E_s[V_i]}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\binom{0}{0},
\Sigma_{UV}
\right).\]
Apply the delta method to \(g(u,v)=u/v\). Its gradient at \((0,E_s[V_i])\) is
\[\nabla g(0,E_s[V_i])=\left(\frac1{E_s[V_i]},\ 0\right).\]
Therefore
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=\sqrt n\,g(\bar U_n,\bar V_n)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,\ \nabla g\,\Sigma_{UV}\,\nabla g^\top\right).
\]
Because only the first coordinate of the gradient is nonzero,
\[\nabla g\,\Sigma_{UV}\,\nabla g^\top
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s(U_i)}{(E_s[V_i])^2}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(w_Z(\mathbf X)\{\tau(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}\right)}{\left(E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.\]
This is the displayed variance. \(\square\)

\textbf{Benchmark reading note.} The abstract exact-share theorem below remains the general theorem for this cell. The fully closed benchmark-style version, proved under the primitive sufficient route, is Corollary~5.6B immediately after the theorem and its expanded proof.

\textbf{Theorem 5.6} (Exact centered known-score limit for retrospective
PATE). Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.2, and 2.4, and Closure Input 5.4,
\[\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\]
If additionally a bias-corrected estimator
\(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}\) satisfies
\[\sqrt n\left(B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}-\widehat B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)\to_p 0,\]
then
\[\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\]

\textbf{Proof.} By Lemma 5.2,
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]
The heterogeneity term is asymptotically normal by Lemma 5.5. For
the residual term, define the martingale-difference array \[\xi_{n,j}
:=
\frac{\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\quad\text{for }Z_j=1,\] and \[\xi_{n,j}
:=
-\frac{\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\quad\text{for }Z_j=0.\] Lemma 5.3 gives conditional mean zero and the
conditional variance sum. Closure Input 5.4 gives the variance limit and
the Lyapunov condition. Therefore
\[\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\] Now compute the
asymptotic covariance between the heterogeneity term and the residual
term. Because
\[E\!\left[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathbf X_1,Z_1,\dots,\mathbf X_n,Z_n,\mathcal J\right]=0,\]
and the heterogeneity term is measurable with respect to the same
sigma-field, we have
\[E\!\left[H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right]=0.\]
Hence the limiting variance is the sum of the two limiting variances.
Slutsky's theorem yields the centered CLT. The bias-corrected conclusion
follows by replacing \(B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) with an
asymptotically equivalent bias estimate. \(\square\)

\medskip
\textbf{Expanded chain-of-equation proof of Theorem 5.6.}

Write
\[
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}:=\sqrt n\,H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\qquad
M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}:=\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
By Lemma~5.2,
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
=
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}.
\]
Therefore it is enough to analyze the two addends separately and then
combine them.

\subsubsection{Step 1. The heterogeneity block}\label{step-1.-the-heterogeneity-block}

By definition,
\[
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\sqrt n\,\frac{\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}}{\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)}.
\]
Lemma~5.5 proves that
\[
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}\right).
\]
Also, \(W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}\) depends only on the sampled covariates,
treatment assignments, survey weights, and the target function
\(\tau(\mathbf X_i)\), so it is \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\)-measurable.

\subsubsection{Step 2. The matched unit-residual block as a martingale sum}\label{step-2.-the-matched unit-residual-block-as-a-martingale-sum}

Define, for each sampled unit \(j\),
\[
\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}
:=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=1\}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=0\}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].
\]
Then
\[
M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\sum_{j=1}^n \xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}.
\]
Now verify conditional mean zero one component at a time. Conditional on
\(\mathcal F_{n,0}\), the coefficients are deterministic. Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]
&=
\frac{\mathbf 1\{Z_j=1\}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&\quad-
\frac{\mathbf 1\{Z_j=0\}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&=
0-0\\
&=0,
\end{aligned}
\]
because
\[
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=1\right]=0,
\qquad
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0\right]=0.
\]
Hence the partial sums of the array are a martingale with respect to the
filtration that starts from \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\) and then reveals the
residuals in a deterministic unit order.

\subsubsection{Step 3. Predictable quadratic variation and Lyapunov remainder}\label{step-3.-predictable-quadratic-variation-and-lyapunov-remainder}

Lemma~5.3 gives the exact conditional quadratic variation:
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
&:=
\sum_{j=1}^n E\!\left[\left(\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}\right)^2\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Closure Input~5.4 states exactly that
\[
Q_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}\to_p V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\]
and also that the Lyapunov remainder of order \(2+\delta\) vanishes:
\[
\sum_{j=1}^n E\!\left[\left|\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}\right|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\to_p 0.
\]
Apply Lemma~4.2 to obtain
\[
M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
Because the limit variance is deterministic, Imported Lemma~4.2B
upgrades this to stable convergence with respect to \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\).

\subsubsection{Step 4. Combine the two blocks without adding an orthogonality assumption}\label{step-4.-combine-the-two-blocks-without-adding-an-orthogonality-assumption}

For every real \(t\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}})}\right]
&=
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}e^{itM_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}\right]\\
&=
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Imported Lemma~4.2B gives
\[
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)
\to_p
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
The inner conditional characteristic function is bounded in absolute
value by one, so dominated convergence yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
&E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}})}\right]
-
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}\right]\\
&\to 0.
\end{aligned}
\]
Lemma~5.5 gives
\[
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}}\right]
\to
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}\right).
\]
Therefore
\[
E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}})}\right]
\to
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2\{V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\}\right).
\]
Lévy's continuity theorem now yields
\[
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
Returning to the first display in this subsection proves
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]

\subsubsection{Step 5. Bias-corrected version}\label{step-5.-bias-corrected-version}

If
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)\to_p 0,
\]
then
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right)\\
&=
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
+
\sqrt n\left(B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}-\widehat B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)\\
&=
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)+o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Slutsky's theorem preserves the same Gaussian limit. This closes the
detailed proof of Theorem~5.6.
\(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 5.6A} (Retrospective PATE variance written on the double-score scale).
Under the assumptions of Theorem~5.6,
\[V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  w_Z(\mathbf X)
  \bigl\{
    m_{s,1}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))
    -m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
    -\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
  \bigr\}
\right)}{\left(E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.
\]
The retrospective PATE bias discrepancy may likewise be written as
\[\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)
=
m_{s,1-Z_i}(D_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i))-
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}m_{s,1-Z_i}(D_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)).\]

\textbf{Proof.} Lemma~3.6A gives
\[\tau(\mathbf X)=m_{s,1}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\qquad\text{a.s.}\]
Substitute this identity into the definition of \(V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}\).
The bias identity is the second display of Lemma~3.6A with the
retrospective PATE coefficients. \(\square\)



\textbf{Corollary 5.6B} (Retrospective PATE exact centered known-score CLT under the primitive sufficient route). Assume Assumptions~2.1, 2.2, and~2.4. Assume further that Proposition~4.8C holds on both matched-arm score spaces \(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X)\) and \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\), and that Proposition~5.4A holds. Then all closure requirements of Theorem~5.6 are satisfied. Consequently,
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and if a bias-corrected exact estimator removes \(B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
with \(V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) given by Proposition~5.4A.

\textbf{Proof.} Proposition~4.8C delivers Closure Input~4.7A on both matched-arm score spaces, so Corollary~4.9 gives the exact first- and second-moment matching-frequency limits required by Sections~5.1--5.3. Proposition~5.4A delivers Closure Input~5.4. Lemma~5.5 therefore gives the heterogeneity CLT and Theorem~5.6 gives the centered known-score exact CLT. The bias-corrected conclusion is the second part of Theorem~5.6. \(\square\)


\subsection{5.5 Estimated-score retrospective PATE results}\label{retrospective-pate-estimated-score-results}

For the retrospective PATE cell, let
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
:=
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Let \(\widehat\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}\) collect the retrospective survey-weighted propensity-score estimator together with the two prognostic-score estimators, and let \(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n,d}\) denote its midpoint discretization on cubes of side length \(d/\sqrt n\).

\textbf{Theorem 5.7} (Retrospective PATE estimated-score limits, fixed law and moving target). Assume Theorem~5.6, Section~10.1B, and Closure Input~10.1 specialized to the retrospective PATE cell.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item \textbf{Fixed-law scientific target.}
Under Proposition~10.3,
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
&=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
-\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
By the discretization transfer in Imported Lemma~10.1B, the same limit holds with \(\widehat\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}\) in place of \(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n,d}\).

\item \textbf{Score-indexed moving-target extension.}
Under Proposition~10.5A specialized to the retrospective PATE cell,
\[
\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
=
\frac{
E_{s,\theta}\!\left[
w_Z(\mathbf X)
\left\{
m_{s,1,\theta}(\mathbf D_{1,\theta}(\mathbf X))
-
m_{s,0,\theta}(\mathbf D_{0,\theta}(\mathbf X))
\right\}
\right]
}{
E_{s,\theta}[w_Z(\mathbf X)]
},
\]
and
\[
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}
:=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
\right|_{\theta=\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^\ast}.
\]
Then
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
&=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
+
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}
+
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\sqrt n\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
-
\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
\right\}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\right).
\]
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Proof.} Theorem~5.6 gives the retrospective PATE known-score variance
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
=
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Apply Theorem~10.6 with the cell label \(A=(R,\mathrm{PATE})\). Under fixed law, Proposition~10.3 gives \(\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}=0\), so the first display follows with variance
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
-\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},1}.
\]
Under the separate Yang--Zhang-style score-indexed route of Proposition~10.5A, the reduced target map may move with \(\theta\), so the second adjustment block is added one term at a time:
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
+
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}.
\]
The discretized statement is exactly Theorem~10.6 specialized to the retrospective PATE cell, and Imported Lemma~10.1B transfers the same limit to \(\widehat\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 5.7A} (Why retrospective PATE is not forced to have zero second block under the moving-target route). The scientific fixed-law PATE has derivative zero, so the fixed-law theorem above has no second adjustment block. The Yang--Zhang-style reduced-score target map \(\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)\), however, need not be flat even in a PATE cell. Therefore the moving-target extension can carry a nonzero \(\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATE},2}\) even though the structural PATE itself is fixed.

\subsection{5.6 Linearization-based bootstrap for retrospective PATE}\label{retrospective-pate-bootstrap-main-text}

For each bootstrap replicate \(b=1,\ldots,B\), draw
\[
(m_1^{\ast,b},\ldots,m_n^{\ast,b})
\sim
\mathrm{Multinomial}(n;1/n,\ldots,1/n),
\]
and define the replicated retrospective survey weight
\[
w_i^{\ast,b}:=m_i^{\ast,b}w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Refit the retrospective propensity-score and arm-specific prognostic-score estimators with these replication weights exactly as in Appendix~O.1, and let \(\widehat\mu_z^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}\) denote the corresponding bias-correction regressions on the replicated score values.

Define the retrospective PATE linear term
\[
\varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATE},i}
=
w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}
+
(2Z_i-1)\Bigl\{
w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)
+
K_{n,Z_i}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(i)
\Bigr\}\varepsilon_{Z_i,i}.
\]
The retrospective PATE bootstrap replicate is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_i^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n
\Big[
& w_i^{\ast,b}
\Big\{
\widehat\mu_1^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X_i))
-
\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))
\Big\}\\
&\quad+
m_i^{\ast,b}(2Z_i-1)
\Bigl\{
w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)+K_{n,Z_i}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(i)
\Bigr\}
\Bigl\{
Y_i-\widehat\mu_{Z_i}^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i))
\Bigr\}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{Proposition 5.8} (Retrospective PATE bootstrap linearization and variance target). Under the conditions of Theorem~5.7 and the bootstrap first-step replication conditions recorded in Appendix~O.1,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}}
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}
\sum_{i=1}^n
(m_i^{\ast,b}-1)
\left\{
\varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATE},i}
-
\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)
\right\}
+
o_{p^\ast}(1),
\]
where
\[
\widehat N_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}
:=
\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Consequently, conditionally on the original sample,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
\Rightarrow_p
N\!\left(0,V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}\right),
\]
with
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
\]
under the known-score theory,
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
\]
under the fixed-law estimated-score theory, and
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
\]
under the moving-target estimated-score extension.

\textbf{Proof.} This is Proposition~O.1 specialized to the retrospective PATE cell. Change only the outer H\'ajek weight, the exact matching-frequency term, and the replicated first-step estimators. The generic bootstrap linearization in Appendix~O.5 then reduces exactly to the displayed retrospective PATE formula. The three possible variance targets follow by substituting the corresponding retrospective PATE asymptotic linear representation from Theorem~5.6 or Theorem~5.7 into Proposition~O.1. \(\square\)


\section{6. Retrospective PATT with exact one-sided within-match survey-weight-normalized
imputation}\label{retrospective-patt-with-exact-one-sided-within-match survey-weight-normalized-imputation}

\subsection{6.1 Exact estimator and exact matched unit
load}\label{exact-estimator-and-exact-survey-weighted matching-frequency-1}

Focal units are the treated units. Matched units are the control units. Matching
is carried out on the one-sided known-score score space
\[\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_0(\mathbf X)).\] In the known-score one-sided specialization used
here, \[\Psi_0(\mathbf X)=\mu_0(\mathbf X).\] For each treated focal unit \(i\) let
\(\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\) be the set of its \(M\) nearest control matched units on
that score space. Define the exact control-matched unit coefficients
\[\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{w_0(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)} w_0(\mathbf X_k)}\,\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\},
\qquad Z_i=1.\] Define the exact imputed control outcome for treated
focal unit \(i\) by \[\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)
:=
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j,
\qquad Z_i=1.\] The exact retrospective PATT estimator is
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)}.\]
Define the denominator average \[\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}
:=
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n Z_iw_1(\mathbf X_i).\] Define the exact one-sided control
survey-weighted matching frequency \[K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)
\frac{w_0(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)}w_0(\mathbf X_k)}\,
\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\}.\]

\textbf{Lemma 6.1} (Exact matching-frequency rewrite for retrospective PATT).
The exact retrospective PATT estimator can be written as
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
\sum_{j:Z_j=1} w_1(\mathbf X_j)Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Expand one object at a time: \[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1} w_1(\mathbf X_j)Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Divide by
\[\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)=n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}.\] \(\square\)

\subsection{6.2 Exact decomposition and exact conditional
variance}\label{exact-decomposition-and-exact-conditional-variance}

\textbf{Lemma 6.2} (Exact decomposition for retrospective PATT). Define
\[H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\},\]
\[B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)
\left[
\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
\right],\] and \[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid 1,i}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\] Then
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the exact estimator and subtract the target:
\[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\bigl[Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\bigr].
\end{aligned}\] Add and subtract \(\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)\): \[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\bigl[Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)\bigr].
\end{aligned}\] For treated focal unit \(i\), \[\begin{aligned}
Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{R,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)
&=
\mu_{1\mid 1}(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1\mid 1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\{\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)+\varepsilon_{0,j}\}-\mu_{1\mid 1}(\mathbf X_i)+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
+
\varepsilon_{1\mid 1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] Substitute this identity into the display above and
re-index the matched unit residual term by control matched unit \(j\) exactly as in
Lemma 6.1. This yields the displayed decomposition. \(\square\)

\textbf{Lemma 6.3} (Exact conditional residual variance for
retrospective PATT). Let \(\mathcal F_n\) be the sigma-field generated
by all covariates, treatments, matched sets, and exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
coefficients in the retrospective PATT problem. Then
\[E\!\left[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]=0,\]
and
\[\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)^2\sigma_{1\mid 1}^2(\mathbf X_i)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].\]

\textbf{Proof.} We expand every step. Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\),
the coefficients \[w_1(\mathbf X_i),
\qquad
K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j),
\qquad
\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}\] are deterministic. Therefore
\[E\!\left[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]
=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
E\!\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\Bigm| \mathcal F_n
\right].\] Using \[E(\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\mid \mathbf X_i,Z_i=1)=0,
\qquad
E(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0)=0,\] we obtain
\[E\!\left[E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]=0.\]
Now expand the conditional variance: \[\begin{aligned}
&\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)\\
&=
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right]
\Bigm|\mathcal F_n
\right)\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2}}
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\Bigm|\mathcal F_n
\right)\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2}}
\Biggl[
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\Bigm|\mathcal F_n\right)
+
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}\Bigm|\mathcal F_n\right)\\
&\hspace{5em}
-2\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i},\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}\Bigm|\mathcal F_n\right)
\Biggr].
\end{aligned}\] Because treated residuals and control residuals are
conditionally mean-zero and conditionally independent across units once
\(X\) and \(Z\) are fixed, the cross-covariance term is zero. Hence
\[\begin{aligned}
&\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\mid \mathcal F_n)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X_i)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}\] This is exactly the displayed formula. \(\square\)

\subsection{6.3 Exact known-score
asymptotics}\label{exact-known-score-asymptotics-1}

\textbf{Closure Input 6.4} (Retrospective PATT exact residual
stabilization). The predictable quadratic variation converges in probability to a finite nonzero limit \(V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\). Proposition~6.4A below gives one optional equal-share validation route to this closure condition through Corollary~4.8A and Proposition~4.12C:

\[\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w_1(\mathbf X_i)^2\sigma_{1\mid 1}^2(\mathbf X_i)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right]
\to_p
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}},\] Moreover, for some \(\delta>0\),
\[\frac{1}{n^{1+\delta/2}\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2+\delta}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}|w_1(\mathbf X_i)|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_i,Z_i=1\bigr]
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}|K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{0,j}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0\bigr]
\right]
\to_p 0.\]


\textbf{Proposition 6.4A} (Optional equal-share subclass implying Closure Input~6.4). Assume Corollary~4.11, Lemma~4.8B, Corollary~4.8A, and Proposition~4.12C on the one-sided control matched-arm score space \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\). Then Closure Input~6.4 holds with
\[
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=1\}w_1(\mathbf X)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X)\right]
+
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=0\}\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X)\kappa_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\right]
}{\left(E_s[Zw_1(\mathbf X)]\right)^2},
\]
where
\[
\kappa_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{eq}}(d)
=
\frac1M\frac{f_{1,0}^{(2),T}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}
+
\frac{\alpha(M,2)}{M^2}
\left\{\frac{f_{1,0}^{(1),T}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}\right\}^2.
\]

\textbf{Proof.} The treated part of the predictable quadratic variation in Closure Input~6.4 already involves the directly observed treated outcomes and therefore needs no matching-geometry closure. For the control part, Lemma~4.8B and Corollary~4.8A collapse the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized share to \(1/M\), and Corollary~4.11 then yields the displayed score-space second-moment function \(\kappa_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{eq}}(d)\). Proposition~4.12C transfers that score-space equal-share closure to the sample quadratic-variation average. The Lyapunov display follows in the same way. \(\square\)


\textbf{Lemma 6.5} (Retrospective PATT heterogeneity CLT). Assume
\[E_s\left[\left|Zw_1(\mathbf X)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}\right|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty,
\qquad
E_s\left[|Zw_1(\mathbf X)|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty\]
for some \(\delta>0\). Then
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}\right),\]
where
\[V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}
:=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(Zw_1(\mathbf X)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}\right)}{\left(E_s[Zw_1(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Define
\[U_i:=Z_iw_1(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\},
\qquad
V_i:=Z_iw_1(\mathbf X_i).\]
Then \(H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=\bar U_n/\bar V_n\). Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.2, and 2.5, the selected sample is i.i.d., \(E_s[U_i]=0\), and \(E_s[V_i]>0\). The bivariate CLT for \((\bar U_n,\bar V_n)\) and the delta method applied to \(g(u,v)=u/v\) at \((0,E_s[V_i])\) give
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s(U_i)}{(E_s[V_i])^2}\right),\]
which is the displayed variance. \(\square\)

\textbf{Benchmark reading note.} The abstract exact-share theorem below remains the general theorem for this cell. The fully closed benchmark-style version, proved under the primitive sufficient route, is Corollary~6.6B immediately after the theorem and its expanded proof.

\textbf{Theorem 6.6} (Exact centered known-score limit for retrospective
PATT). Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.2, and 2.5, and Closure Input 6.4,
\[\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\]
If a bias-corrected exact estimator removes
\(B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then
the same variance is the limit variance of the bias-corrected exact
estimator.

\textbf{Proof.} Use the exact decomposition in Lemma 6.2. The
heterogeneity term is asymptotically normal by Lemma 6.5. The
residual term is asymptotically normal by Lemma 4.2, applied to the
one-sided survey-weighted matching-frequency residual array with conditional variance given by
Lemma 6.3 and Lyapunov condition given by Closure Input 6.4. The covariance
between the heterogeneity term and the residual term is zero because the
heterogeneity term is measurable with respect to the covariates,
treatments, and matching graph, while the conditional mean of the
residual term given the same sigma-field is zero. The bias-corrected
statement follows by Slutsky. \(\square\)

\medskip
\textbf{Expanded chain-of-equation proof of Theorem 6.6.}

Write
\[
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}:=\sqrt n\,H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\qquad
M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}:=\sqrt n\,E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Lemma~6.2 gives the exact decomposition
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
=
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}.
\]

\subsubsection{Step 1. The heterogeneity block for retrospective PATT}\label{step-1.-the-heterogeneity-block-for-retrospective-patt}

Lemma~6.5 shows that
\[
W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}\right).
\]
As in the retrospective PATE case, the block depends only on sampled
covariates, treatment assignments, survey weights, and the matching
graph, so it is \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\)-measurable.

\subsubsection{Step 2. The matched unit-residual block for retrospective PATT}\label{step-2.-the-matched unit-residual-block-for-retrospective-patt}

Define
\[
\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}
:=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=1\}w_1(\mathbf X_j)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,j}
-
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=0\}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].
\]
Then
\[
M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}=\sum_{j=1}^n \xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}.
\]
Conditional on \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\), the coefficients are fixed. Hence
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]
&=
\frac{\mathbf 1\{Z_j=1\}w_1(\mathbf X_j)}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{1\mid1,j}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&\quad-
\frac{\mathbf 1\{Z_j=0\}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&=0,
\end{aligned}
\]
because
\[
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{1\mid1,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=1\right]=0,
\qquad
E\!\left[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0\right]=0.
\]
Thus the array is a martingale-difference array.

Lemma~6.3 gives the exact predictable quadratic variation:
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}
&:=
\sum_{j=1}^n E\!\left[\left(\xi_{n,j}^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}\right)^2\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}w_1(\mathbf X_j)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Closure Input~6.4 states that
\[
Q_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}\to_p V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\]
and that the corresponding Lyapunov remainder tends to zero. Therefore
Lemma~4.2 gives
\[
M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and Imported Lemma~4.2B upgrades this to stable convergence relative to
\(\mathcal F_{n,0}\).

\subsubsection{Step 3. Combine the two retrospective PATT blocks}\label{step-3.-combine-the-two-retrospective-patt-blocks}

For any real \(t\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}})}\right]
&=
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}}
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Imported Lemma~4.2B gives
\[
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)
\to_p
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
Use bounded convergence exactly as in the retrospective PATE proof immediately above to obtain
\[
E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+M_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}})}\right]
\to
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2\{V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\}\right).
\]
Hence
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
The bias-corrected version follows from the same Slutsky step as in
the retrospective PATE proof immediately above.
\(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 6.6A} (Retrospective PATT variance written on the one-sided score scale).
Under the assumptions of Theorem~6.6,
\[V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  Zw_1(\mathbf X)
  \bigl\{
    \mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
  \bigr\}
\right)}{\left(E_s[Zw_1(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.
\]
The one-sided bias discrepancy may be written as
\[\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
=
m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))-
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{R,(0),\mathrm{ex}}m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_j)).\]

\textbf{Proof.} By Lemma~3.6A,
\[\tau_T(\mathbf X)=\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\qquad\text{a.s.}\]
Substitute this identity into the definition of
\(V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}\). The bias identity is the one-sided display of
Lemma~3.6A with the retrospective PATT coefficients. \(\square\)



\textbf{Corollary 6.6B} (Retrospective PATT exact centered known-score CLT under the primitive sufficient route). Assume Assumptions~2.1, 2.2, and~2.5. Assume further that Proposition~4.8C holds on the one-sided control matched-arm score space \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\), and that Proposition~6.4A holds. Then all closure requirements of Theorem~6.6 are satisfied. Consequently,
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and if a bias-corrected exact estimator removes \(B_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then the same variance is the limiting variance of the bias-corrected exact estimator.

\textbf{Proof.} Proposition~4.8C delivers Closure Input~4.7A on the one-sided control matched-arm score space, so Corollary~4.11 supplies the exact matching-frequency moment limits used in Section~6. Proposition~6.4A delivers Closure Input~6.4. Lemma~6.5 then gives the heterogeneity CLT and Theorem~6.6 gives the centered known-score exact CLT. \(\square\)


\subsection{6.4 Estimated-score retrospective PATT results}\label{retrospective-patt-estimated-score-results}

For the retrospective PATT cell, let
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
:=
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Let \(\widehat\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}\) collect the retrospective survey-weighted propensity-score estimator together with the one-sided control prognostic-score estimator, and let \(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n,d}\) denote its midpoint discretization on cubes of side length \(d/\sqrt n\).

\textbf{Theorem 6.7} (Retrospective PATT estimated-score limits, fixed law and moving target). Assume Theorem~6.6, Section~10.1B, and Closure Input~10.1 specialized to the retrospective PATT cell.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item \textbf{Fixed-law scientific target.}
Under Proposition~10.3,
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
&=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
-\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
By the discretization transfer in Imported Lemma~10.1B, the same limit holds with \(\widehat\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}\) in place of \(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n,d}\).

\item \textbf{Score-indexed moving-target extension.}
Under Proposition~10.5A specialized to the retrospective PATT cell,
\[
\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
=
\frac{
E_{s,\theta}\!\left[
Zw_1(\mathbf X)
\left\{
\mu_{1\mid1,\theta}(\mathbf X)
-
m_{s,0,\theta}(\mathbf D_{0,\theta}(\mathbf X))
\right\}
\right]
}{
E_{s,\theta}[Zw_1(\mathbf X)]
},
\]
and
\[
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}
:=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
\right|_{\theta=\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^\ast}.
\]
Then
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
&=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
+
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}
+
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\sqrt n\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
-
\tau_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\bar\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
\right\}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\right).
\]
If the stronger full-ignorability shortcut of Corollary~10.5 is imposed, then \(\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}\) may be replaced by the explicit quotient derivative given there.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Proof.} Theorem~6.6 gives
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
=
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Apply Theorem~10.6 with the cell label \(A=(R,\mathrm{PATT})\). Proposition~10.3 yields the fixed-law result. Proposition~10.5A yields the one-sided score-indexed moving-target derivative and therefore the second adjustment block
\[
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}^\top
\Sigma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}.
\]
The discretized statement is exactly the retrospective PATT specialization of Theorem~10.6, and Imported Lemma~10.1B transfers it to \(\widehat\theta_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 6.7A} (Two distinct sources of the second block in retrospective PATT). Under the scientific fixed-law interpretation, \(\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}=0\). Under the Yang--Zhang-style score-indexed route, \(\gamma_{R,\mathrm{PATT},2}\) can be nonzero because the reduced-score target map depends on the fitted propensity and prognostic scores. Under the stronger full-ignorability changing-law shortcut, a second source also appears through the treated-population weighting ratio \(E[\tau(\mathbf X)e_\theta(\mathbf X)]/E[e_\theta(\mathbf X)]\).

\subsection{6.5 Linearization-based bootstrap for retrospective PATT}\label{retrospective-patt-bootstrap-main-text}

For each bootstrap replicate \(b=1,\ldots,B\), draw
\[
(m_1^{\ast,b},\ldots,m_n^{\ast,b})
\sim
\mathrm{Multinomial}(n;1/n,\ldots,1/n),
\]
and define
\[
w_i^{\ast,b}:=m_i^{\ast,b}w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Refit the retrospective propensity-score estimator and the control-side prognostic-score estimator exactly as in Appendix~O.3, and let \(\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}\) denote the replicated bias-correction regression on the control score space.

Define the retrospective PATT linear term
\[
\varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATT},i}
=
\begin{cases}
w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\}, & Z_i=1,\\[1mm]
-K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(i)\varepsilon_{0,i}, & Z_i=0.
\end{cases}
\]
The retrospective PATT bootstrap replicate is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\ast,b}
=
\frac{
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}
\{Y_i-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))\}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0} m_j^{\ast,b}K_{n,0}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
\{Y_j-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_j))\}
}{
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}
}.
\]

\textbf{Proposition 6.8} (Retrospective PATT bootstrap linearization and variance target). Under the conditions of Theorem~6.7 and the bootstrap first-step replication conditions recorded in Appendix~O.3,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}}
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}
\sum_{i=1}^n
(m_i^{\ast,b}-1)
\left\{
\varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATT},i}
-
\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}Z_iw_1(\mathbf X_i)
\right\}
+
o_{p^\ast}(1),
\]
where
\[
\widehat N_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Consequently, conditionally on the original sample,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
\Rightarrow_p
N\!\left(0,V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}\right),
\]
with
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
\]
under the known-score theory,
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
\]
under the fixed-law estimated-score theory, and
\[
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
\]
under the moving-target estimated-score extension.

\textbf{Proof.} This is Proposition~O.1 specialized to the retrospective PATT cell. Change only the treated-side denominator, the one-sided control matching-frequency term, and the replicated one-sided score estimator. The cell-specific bootstrap linearization and the three large-sample variance targets follow exactly as in Appendix~O.3--O.5. \(\square\)


\section{7. Prospective PATE with exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
imputation}\label{prospective-pate-with-exact-within-match survey-weight-normalized-imputation}

Under prospective sampling the common survey weight is \(w(\mathbf X)\).
Matching is still done arm by arm on the known-score score spaces
\[\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_z(\mathbf X))=(e(\mathbf X),\mu_z(\mathbf X)),\qquad z\in\{0,1\}.\]

\subsection{7.1 Exact estimator, exact survey-weighted matching frequencies, and exact
matching-frequency
representation}\label{exact-estimator-exact-survey-weighted matching-frequencys-and-exact-matching-frequency-representation}

For each focal unit \(i\) in arm \(1-z\), let \(\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_z}(i)\) be the
set of its \(M\) nearest matched units in arm \(z\) on \(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)\).

Define the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized within-match coefficient by
\[\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(z),\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{w(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_z}(i)} w(\mathbf X_k)}\,\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_z}(i)\},
\qquad j=1,\dots,n,\] when \(Z_i=1-z\). Define it to equal \(0\) when
\(Z_i=z\).

Define the exact imputed counterfactuals
\[\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(z)
:=
\begin{cases}
Y_i,& Z_i=z,\\[4pt]
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(z),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j,& Z_i=1-z.
\end{cases}\] Then the exact prospective PATE estimator is
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)\bigl\{\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\bigr\}}
{\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)}.\] Define the denominator mean
\[\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i).\] Define
the exact survey-weighted matching frequencies \[K_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}
 w(\mathbf X_i)
 \frac{w(\mathbf X_j)\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)\}}
 {\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)} w(\mathbf X_k)},
\qquad Z_j=1,\] and \[K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}
 w(\mathbf X_i)
 \frac{w(\mathbf X_j)\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\}}
 {\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)} w(\mathbf X_k)},
\qquad Z_j=0.\] Define the exact matched unit coefficients
\[\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=w(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j),\] and
\[\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=w(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j).\]

\textbf{Proposition 7.1} (Prospective PATE exact matching-frequency
representation). The exact prospective PATE estimator admits the exact
matching-frequency representation
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
}{\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the numerator and expand one arm at a time:
\[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)\bigl\{\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\bigr\}\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Regroup the treated-matched unit imputation term by matched unit index
\(j\): \[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w(\mathbf X_i)
\frac{w(\mathbf X_j)\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)\}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_1}(i)}w(\mathbf X_k)}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}K_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Regroup the control-matched unit imputation term by matched unit index
\(j\): \[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)
\frac{w(\mathbf X_j)\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)}w(\mathbf X_k)}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Substitute both identities back into the numerator:
\[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)\bigl\{\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\bigr\}\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\bigl\{w(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\bigr\}Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\bigl\{w(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\bigr\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Now divide by
\[\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)=n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}.\] \(\square\)

\subsection{7.2 Exact decomposition and exact conditional residual
variance}\label{exact-decomposition-and-exact-conditional-residual-variance}

Define \[H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\},\]
\[B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)(2Z_i-1)
\left[
\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)
\right],\] and \[E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\]

\textbf{Lemma 7.2} (Prospective PATE exact decomposition). The exact
prospective PATE estimator satisfies
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
=
H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]
The discrepancy sign in \(B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) is
\textbf{focal unit minus matched unit} in both arms.

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the exact prospective estimator and subtract
the target: \[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\left[Y_i-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right]
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=0}w(\mathbf X_i)\left[\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j-Y_i-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\right].
\end{aligned}\] For a treated focal unit \(i\) with \(Z_i=1\), substitute
\(Y_i=\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1,i}\) and
\(Y_j=\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)+\varepsilon_{0,j}\): \[\begin{aligned}
&Y_i-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\\
&=
\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1,i}
-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}.
\end{aligned}\] Add and subtract \(\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\) and then \(\tau(\mathbf X_i)\)
one step at a time: \[\begin{aligned}
