&\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
+\varepsilon_{1,i}
-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}
-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\\
&=
\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
+\varepsilon_{1,i}
-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}
+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j).
\end{aligned}\] For a control focal unit \(i\) with \(Z_i=0\), substitute
\(Y_j=\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)+\varepsilon_{1,j}\) and
\(Y_i=\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{0,i}\): \[\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}Y_j-Y_i-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\\
&=
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)
+\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}
-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\varepsilon_{0,i}
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}.
\end{aligned}\] Add and subtract \(\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)\) and then \(\tau(\mathbf X_i)\)
one step at a time: \[\begin{aligned}
&\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
+\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}
-\varepsilon_{0,i}
-\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)\\
&=
\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
+\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}
-\varepsilon_{0,i}
-\left[\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)\right].
\end{aligned}\] Multiply the treated identity by \(w(\mathbf X_i)\), multiply
the control identity by \(w(\mathbf X_i)\), sum over all \(i\), and divide by
\(n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}\). The heterogeneity terms combine into
\(H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\). The discrepancy terms combine
into \(B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) with the displayed
focal-unit-minus-matched-unit sign. The residual terms regroup matched unit by matched unit
exactly as in Proposition 7.1, producing
\(E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Lemma 7.3} (Prospective PATE exact conditional residual
variance). Let \(\mathcal F_n\) be the sigma-field generated by all
covariates, treatments, matched sets, and exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
coefficients in the prospective PATE problem. Then
\[E\!\left[E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]=0,\]
and
\[\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].\]

\textbf{Proof.} Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\), the exact matched unit
coefficients are deterministic. Also,
\[E[\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=1]=0,
\qquad
E[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0]=0.\] Therefore \[\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)E(\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)E(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=0.
\end{aligned}\] Now write the scaled residual term explicitly:
\[\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\] Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\), the residuals are
independent across sampled units, so the cross terms vanish. Therefore
\[\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\Bigm|\mathcal F_n
\right)\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{1,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}\] \(\square\)

\textbf{Proposition 7.4} (Prospective PATE exact bias order). Under
Assumptions 2.3, 2.4, and 4.4,
\[B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=O_p(n^{-1/2}).\]

\textbf{Proof.} By Lemma 4.5, on arm \(z\) we have
\[\left|\mu_z(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(z),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_z(\mathbf X_j)\right|\le L_z\rho_{n,z}(i).\]
Therefore, \[\begin{aligned}
|B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}|
&\le
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)L_{1-Z_i}\rho_{n,1-Z_i}(i).
\end{aligned}\] Because \(\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}\to_p E_s[w(\mathbf X)]>0\)
and because the common survey weight has finite moments under
prospective positivity, the right-hand side has the same stochastic
order as the average matching radius. By Lemma 4.3 that order is
\(O_p(n^{-1/2})\). Hence
\[B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=O_p(n^{-1/2}).\] \(\square\)

\subsection{7.3 Exact known-score
asymptotics}\label{exact-known-score-asymptotics-2}

\textbf{Closure Input 7.5} (Prospective PATE exact residual stabilization). The predictable quadratic variation converges in probability to a finite nonzero limit \(V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\). Proposition~7.5A below gives one optional equal-share validation route to this closure condition through Corollary~4.8A and Proposition~4.12C:

\[\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^{2}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^{2}\sigma_1^{2}(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^{2}\sigma_0^{2}(\mathbf X_j)
\right]
\to_p V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\] Moreover, for some
\(\delta>0\),
\[\frac{1}{n^{1+\delta/2}\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^{2+\delta}}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}|\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{1,j}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=1\bigr]
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}|\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{0,j}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0\bigr]
\right]
\to_p 0.\]


\textbf{Proposition 7.5A} (Optional equal-share subclass implying Closure Input~7.5). Assume Corollary~4.10, Lemma~4.8B, Corollary~4.8A, and Proposition~4.12C on both matched-arm score spaces \(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X)\) and \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\). Then Closure Input~7.5 holds with
\[
V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=1\}\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X)\kappa_{1,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))\right]
+
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=0\}\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X)\kappa_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\right]
}{\left(E_s[w(\mathbf X)]\right)^2},
\]
where
\[
\kappa_{z,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{eq}}(d)
=
\frac1M\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(2),P}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}
+
\frac{\alpha(M,2)}{M^2}
\left\{\frac{f_{1-z,z}^{(1),P}(d)}{f_{z,z}^s(d)}\right\}^2,
\qquad z\in\{0,1\}.
\]

\textbf{Proof.} The proof is identical to that of Proposition~5.4A, with Corollary~4.10 in place of Corollary~4.9 and the common prospective weight in place of the retrospective arm-specific weights. \(\square\)


\textbf{Lemma 7.6} (Prospective PATE heterogeneity CLT). Assume
\[E_s\left[\left|w(\mathbf X)\{\tau(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}\right|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty,
\qquad
E_s\left[|w(\mathbf X)|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty\]
for some \(\delta>0\). Then
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}\right),\]
where
\[V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}
:=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(w(\mathbf X)\{\tau(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}\right)}{\left(E_s[w(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Define \(U_i:=w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}\) and \(V_i:=w(\mathbf X_i)\). Then \(H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=\bar U_n/\bar V_n\). Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.3, and 2.4, \(E_s[U_i]=0\) and \(E_s[V_i]>0\). The bivariate CLT and the delta method at \((0,E_s[V_i])\) imply
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s(U_i)}{(E_s[V_i])^2}\right),\]
which is exactly the displayed variance. \(\square\)

\textbf{Benchmark reading note.} The abstract exact-share theorem below remains the general theorem for this cell. The fully closed benchmark-style version, proved under the primitive sufficient route, is Corollary~7.7B immediately after the theorem and its expanded proof.

\textbf{Theorem 7.7} (Exact centered known-score limit for prospective PATE).
Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.3, and 2.4, and Closure Input 7.5,
\[\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\]
If a bias-corrected exact estimator removes
\(B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then
the same variance is the limit variance of the bias-corrected exact
estimator.

\textbf{Proof.} By Lemma 7.2,
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]
The heterogeneity term is asymptotically normal by Lemma 7.6.

For the residual term, apply the shared conditional triangular-array CLT
lemma to the array \[\xi_{n,j}
:=
\frac{\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\qquad\text{for }Z_j=1,\] and \[\xi_{n,j}
:=
-\frac{\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\qquad\text{for }Z_j=0.\] Lemma 7.3 gives the conditional mean-zero
property and the exact conditional variance formula. Closure Input 7.5
gives the variance limit and the Lyapunov condition. Hence
\[\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\] The covariance
between \(H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) and
\(E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) is zero because the residual
term has conditional mean zero given the sigma-field generated by the
covariates, treatments, and matching graph, while the heterogeneity term
is measurable with respect to the same sigma-field. Therefore the limit
variance is the sum of the two variance components. The bias-corrected
conclusion follows by Slutsky's theorem. \(\square\)

\medskip
\textbf{Expanded chain-of-equation proof of Theorem 7.7.}

Write
\[
W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}:=\sqrt n\,H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\qquad
M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}:=\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Lemma~7.2 gives
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
=
W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}.
\]

\subsubsection{Step 1. The heterogeneity block for prospective PATE}\label{step-1.-the-heterogeneity-block-for-prospective-pate}

Lemma~7.6 proves
\[
W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}\right).
\]
Again, this block is \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\)-measurable.

\subsubsection{Step 2. The matched unit-residual block for prospective PATE}\label{step-2.-the-matched unit-residual-block-for-prospective-pate}

Define
\[
\xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}
:=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}}
\left[
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=1\}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{1,j}
-
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=0\}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].
\]
Then
\[
M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}=\sum_{j=1}^n \xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}.
\]
Conditional on \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\), the coefficients are fixed, so
\[
E\!\left[\xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]=0.
\]
Lemma~7.3 gives the exact predictable quadratic variation:
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}
&:=
\sum_{j=1}^n E\!\left[\left(\xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}\right)^2\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}\kappa_{n,1}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\kappa_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Closure Input~7.5 states that
\[
Q_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}\to_p V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\]
and that the Lyapunov remainder tends to zero. Hence Lemma~4.2 gives
\[
M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
with stable convergence relative to \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\) by Imported
Lemma~4.2B.

\subsubsection{Step 3. Combine the two prospective PATE blocks}\label{step-3.-combine-the-two-prospective-pate-blocks}

Exactly as before,
\[
E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}})}\right]
=
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}}
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)\right].
\]
The stable martingale limit gives
\[
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)
\to_p
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
while Lemma~7.6 gives
\[
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}}\right]
\to
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}\right).
\]
Therefore
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
The bias-corrected version again follows by Slutsky.
\(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 7.7A} (Prospective PATE variance written on the double-score scale).
Under the assumptions of Theorem~7.7,
\[V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  w(\mathbf X)
  \bigl\{
    m_{s,1}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))
    -m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
    -\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
  \bigr\}
\right)}{\left(E_s[w(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.
\]
The prospective PATE bias discrepancy may likewise be written as
\[\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)
=
m_{s,1-Z_i}(D_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_i))-
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(1-Z_i),\mathrm{ex}}m_{s,1-Z_i}(D_{1-Z_i}(\mathbf X_j)).\]

\textbf{Proof.} Lemma~3.6A gives
\[\tau(\mathbf X)=m_{s,1}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\qquad\text{a.s.}\]
Substitute this identity into the definition of \(V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}\).
The bias identity follows from Lemma~3.6A with the prospective PATE
coefficients. \(\square\)



\textbf{Corollary 7.7B} (Prospective PATE exact centered known-score CLT under the primitive sufficient route). Assume Assumptions~2.1, 2.3, and~2.4. Assume further that Proposition~4.8C holds on both matched-arm score spaces \(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X)\) and \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\), and that Proposition~7.5A holds. Then all closure requirements of Theorem~7.7 are satisfied. Consequently,
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and if a bias-corrected exact estimator removes \(B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then the same variance is the limiting variance of the bias-corrected exact estimator.

\textbf{Proof.} Proposition~4.8C gives Closure Input~4.7A on both matched-arm score spaces, so Corollary~4.10 supplies the exact matching-frequency moment limits used in Section~7. Proposition~7.5A delivers Closure Input~7.5. Lemma~7.6 then gives the heterogeneity CLT and Theorem~7.7 gives the centered known-score exact CLT. \(\square\)


\subsection{7.5 Estimated-score prospective PATE results}\label{prospective-pate-estimated-score-results}

For the prospective PATE cell, let
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
:=
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Let \(\widehat\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}\) collect the prospective propensity-score estimator together with the two prognostic-score estimators, and let \(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d}\) denote its midpoint discretization on cubes of side length \(d/\sqrt n\).

\textbf{Theorem 7.8} (Prospective PATE estimated-score limits, fixed law and moving target). Assume Theorem~7.7, Section~10.1B, and Closure Input~10.1 specialized to the prospective PATE cell.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item \textbf{Fixed-law scientific target.}
Under Proposition~10.3,
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
&=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
-\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},1}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
By the discretization transfer in Imported Lemma~10.1B, the same limit holds with \(\widehat\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}\) in place of \(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d}\).

\item \textbf{Score-indexed moving-target extension.}
Under Proposition~10.5A specialized to the prospective PATE cell,
\[
\tau_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
=
\frac{
E_{s,\theta}\!\left[
w(\mathbf X)
\left\{
m_{s,1,\theta}(\mathbf D_{1,\theta}(\mathbf X))
-
m_{s,0,\theta}(\mathbf D_{0,\theta}(\mathbf X))
\right\}
\right]
}{
E_{s,\theta}[w(\mathbf X)]
},
\]
and
\[
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}
:=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
\tau_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
\right|_{\theta=\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^\ast}.
\]
Then
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
&=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
+
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},1}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},1}
+
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\sqrt n\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
-
\tau_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d})
\right\}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\right).
\]
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Proof.} Theorem~7.7 gives
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
=
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Apply Theorem~10.6 with the cell label \(A=(P,\mathrm{PATE})\). Proposition~10.3 yields the fixed-law result. Proposition~10.5A yields the prospective PATE score-indexed moving-target derivative and the additional variance block
\[
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}.
\]
Imported Lemma~10.1B transfers the result from \(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE},n,d}\) to \(\widehat\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 7.8A} (Prospective PATE and the Yang--Zhang second block). The common prospective sampling weight does not force the moving-target derivative to vanish. The second block is zero only for the scientific fixed-law PATE. Under the score-indexed reduced-target route of Proposition~10.5A, the prospective PATE cell can still carry a nonzero \(\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATE},2}\).

\subsection{7.6 Linearization-based bootstrap for prospective PATE}\label{prospective-pate-bootstrap-main-text}

For each bootstrap replicate \(b=1,\ldots,B\), draw
\[
(m_1^{\ast,b},\ldots,m_n^{\ast,b})
\sim
\mathrm{Multinomial}(n;1/n,\ldots,1/n),
\]
and define
\[
w_i^{\ast,b}:=m_i^{\ast,b}w(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Refit the prospective propensity-score estimator and the two prognostic-score estimators exactly as in Appendix~O.2, and let \(\widehat\mu_z^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}\) denote the replicated bias-correction regressions on the replicated score spaces.

Define the prospective PATE linear term
\[
\varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATE},i}
=
w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}
+
(2Z_i-1)\Bigl\{
w(\mathbf X_i)
+
K_{n,Z_i}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(i)
\Bigr\}\varepsilon_{Z_i,i}.
\]
The prospective PATE bootstrap replicate is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_i^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n
\Big[
& w_i^{\ast,b}
\Big\{
\widehat\mu_1^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X_i))
-
\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))
\Big\}\\
&\quad+
m_i^{\ast,b}(2Z_i-1)
\Bigl\{
w(\mathbf X_i)+K_{n,Z_i}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(i)
\Bigr\}
\Bigl\{
Y_i-\widehat\mu_{Z_i}^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i))
\Bigr\}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{Proposition 7.9} (Prospective PATE bootstrap linearization and variance target). Under the conditions of Theorem~7.8 and the bootstrap first-step replication conditions recorded in Appendix~O.2,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}}
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}
\sum_{i=1}^n
(m_i^{\ast,b}-1)
\left\{
\varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATE},i}
-
\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}w(\mathbf X_i)
\right\}
+
o_{p^\ast}(1),
\]
where
\[
\widehat N_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}
:=
\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Consequently, conditionally on the original sample,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
\Rightarrow_p
N\!\left(0,V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}\right),
\]
with
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
\]
under the known-score theory,
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
\]
under the fixed-law estimated-score theory, and
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
\]
under the moving-target estimated-score extension.

\textbf{Proof.} This is Proposition~O.1 specialized to the prospective PATE cell. Change only the common prospective H\'ajek weight, the prospective exact matching-frequency term, and the replicated first-step estimators. Then Appendix~O.2 and Appendix~O.5 give the displayed bootstrap linearization and the three prospective PATE variance targets. \(\square\)


\section{8. Prospective PATT with exact one-sided within-match survey-weight-normalized
imputation}\label{prospective-patt-with-exact-one-sided-within-match survey-weight-normalized-imputation}

Under prospective sampling the common inverse-inclusion weight is
\(w(\mathbf X)\). Focal units are treated units. Matched units are control units.
Matching is one-sided on the known-score score space
\[\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_0(\mathbf X)).\] In the known-score one-sided specialization used
here, \[\Psi_0(\mathbf X)=\mu_0(\mathbf X).\]

\subsection{8.1 Exact estimator, exact survey-weighted matching frequency, and exact
matching-frequency
representation}\label{exact-estimator-exact-survey-weighted matching-frequency-and-exact-matching-frequency-representation}

For each treated focal unit \(i\) define the exact control-matched unit
coefficient \[\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{w(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)} w(\mathbf X_k)}\,\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\},
\qquad j=1,\dots,n,\] where \(\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\) is the set of the \(M\)
nearest control matched units to treated focal unit \(i\) on \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\). Define
the exact imputed control outcome \[\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)
:=
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}Y_j,
\qquad Z_i=1.\] The exact prospective PATT estimator is
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w(\mathbf X_i)\{Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w(\mathbf X_i)}.\]
Define the denominator mean
\[\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}:=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n Z_i w(\mathbf X_i).\] Define
the exact one-sided survey-weighted matching frequency
\[K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}
 w(\mathbf X_i)
 \frac{w(\mathbf X_j)\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)} w(\mathbf X_k)},
\qquad Z_j=0.\]

\textbf{Proposition 8.1} (Prospective PATT exact matching-frequency
representation). The exact prospective PATT estimator admits the
representation \[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j
}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the numerator: \[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\{Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)\}
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)Y_i
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Regroup the second term by matched unit index \(j\):
\[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}Y_j
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)
\frac{w(\mathbf X_j)\mathbf 1\{j\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)\}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_{\mathbf D_0}(i)}w(\mathbf X_k)}\right\}Y_j\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)Y_j.
\end{aligned}\] Substitute back into the numerator and divide by
\[\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)=n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}.\] \(\square\)

\subsection{8.2 Exact decomposition and exact conditional residual
variance}\label{exact-decomposition-and-exact-conditional-residual-variance-1}

Define \[H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\},\]
\[B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)
\left[\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\right],\]
and \[E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid 1,i}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\]

\textbf{Lemma 8.2} (Prospective PATT exact decomposition). The exact
prospective PATT estimator satisfies
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]
The discrepancy bracket in the bias term is \textbf{focal unit minus
matched unit}.

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the exact estimator numerator after
subtracting the target: \[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\left[Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\right].
\end{aligned}\] Add and subtract \(\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)\): \[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\left[Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)\right].
\end{aligned}\] For treated focal unit \(i\) substitute
\(Y_i=\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\) and
\(Y_j=\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)+\varepsilon_{0,j}\): \[\begin{aligned}
&Y_i-\widehat Y_i^{P,\mathrm{ex}}(0)-\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}
-
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
-
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}
-
\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X_i)+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
+
\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] Substitute this identity into the display above:
\[\begin{aligned}
&n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}\bigl(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\bigr)\\
&=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}
+
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)
\left[\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\right]\\
&\quad+
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}
-
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] Regroup the last term by matched unit index \(j\):
\[\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}\left\{\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\right\}\varepsilon_{0,j}\\
&=
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] Divide by \(n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}\) to obtain the
displayed decomposition. \(\square\)

\textbf{Lemma 8.3} (Prospective PATT exact conditional residual
variance). Let \(\mathcal F_n\) be the sigma-field generated by all
covariates, treatments, matched sets, and exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
coefficients in the prospective PATT problem. Then
\[E\!\left[E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]=0,\]
and
\[\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X_i)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].\]

\textbf{Proof.} Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\), all coefficients are
deterministic. Also, \[E[\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\mid \mathbf X_i,Z_i=1]=0,
\qquad
E[\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0]=0.\] Therefore \[\begin{aligned}
E\!\left[E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right]
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)E(\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\mid \mathcal F_n)
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)E(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=0.
\end{aligned}\] Now write the scaled residual term:
\[\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].\] Conditional on \(\mathcal F_n\), the treated residuals and
control residuals are independent across units, so the cross terms
vanish. Therefore \[\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\mid \mathcal F_n\right)
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\mid \mathcal F_n)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_{0,j}\mid \mathcal F_n)
\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X_i)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}\] \(\square\)

\textbf{Proposition 8.4} (Prospective PATT exact bias order). Under
Assumptions 2.3, 2.5, and 4.4,
\[B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=O_p(n^{-1/2}).\]

\textbf{Proof.} By Lemma 4.5, on the one-sided score space \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\) we
have
\[\left|\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\right|\le L_0\rho_n(i).\]
Therefore, \[\begin{aligned}
|B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}|
&\le
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)L_0\rho_n(i).
\end{aligned}\] Because \(\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}\to_p E_s[Zw(\mathbf X)]>0\)
and the common survey weight has finite moments, the right-hand side has
order \(O_p(n^{-1/2})\) by Lemma 4.3. Hence
\[B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=O_p(n^{-1/2}).\] \(\square\)

\subsection{8.3 Exact known-score
asymptotics}\label{exact-known-score-asymptotics-3}

\textbf{Closure Input 8.5} (Prospective PATT exact residual stabilization). The predictable quadratic variation converges in probability to a finite nonzero limit \(V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\). Proposition~8.5A below gives one optional equal-share validation route to this closure condition through Corollary~4.8A and Proposition~4.12C:

\[\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}w(\mathbf X_i)^{2}\sigma_{1\mid1}^{2}(\mathbf X_i)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^{2}\sigma_0^{2}(\mathbf X_j)
\right]
\to_p V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\] Moreover, for some
\(\delta>0\),
\[\frac{1}{n^{1+\delta/2}\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}^{2+\delta}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1}|w(\mathbf X_i)|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_i,Z_i=1\bigr]
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}|K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)|^{2+\delta}E\bigl[|\varepsilon_{0,j}|^{2+\delta}\mid \mathbf X_j,Z_j=0\bigr]
\right]
\to_p 0.\]


\textbf{Proposition 8.5A} (Optional equal-share subclass implying Closure Input~8.5). Assume Corollary~4.12, Lemma~4.8B, Corollary~4.8A, and Proposition~4.12C on the one-sided control matched-arm score space \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\). Then Closure Input~8.5 holds with
\[
V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=1\}w(\mathbf X)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X)\right]
+
E_s\!\left[\mathbf 1\{Z=0\}\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X)\kappa_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\right]
}{\left(E_s[Zw(\mathbf X)]\right)^2},
\]
where
\[
\kappa_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{eq}}(d)
=
\frac1M\frac{f_{1,0}^{(2),PT}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}
+
\frac{\alpha(M,2)}{M^2}
\left\{\frac{f_{1,0}^{(1),PT}(d)}{f_{0,0}^s(d)}\right\}^2.
\]

\textbf{Proof.} The proof is identical to that of Proposition~6.4A, with Corollary~4.12 in place of Corollary~4.11 and the common prospective weight in place of \(w_1(\mathbf X)\). \(\square\)


\textbf{Lemma 8.6} (Prospective PATT heterogeneity CLT). Assume
\[E_s\left[\left|Zw(\mathbf X)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}\right|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty,
\qquad
E_s\left[|Zw(\mathbf X)|^{2+\delta}\right]<\infty\]
for some \(\delta>0\). Then
\[\sqrt n\,H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}\right),\]
where
\[V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}
:=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(Zw(\mathbf X)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}\right)}{\left(E_s[Zw(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} Define \(U_i:=Z_iw(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}\) and \(V_i:=Z_iw(\mathbf X_i)\). Then \(H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}=\bar U_n/\bar V_n\). Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.3, and 2.5, \(E_s[U_i]=0\) and \(E_s[V_i]>0\). The bivariate CLT and the delta method at \((0,E_s[V_i])\) imply the displayed centered Normal limit with variance \(\operatorname{Var}_s(U_i)/(E_s[V_i])^2\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Benchmark reading note.} The abstract exact-share theorem below remains the general theorem for this cell. The fully closed benchmark-style version, proved under the primitive sufficient route, is Corollary~8.7B immediately after the theorem and its expanded proof.

\textbf{Theorem 8.7} (Exact centered known-score limit for prospective PATT).
Under Assumptions 2.1, 2.3, and 2.5, and Closure Input 8.5,
\[\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\]
If a bias-corrected exact estimator removes
\(B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then
the same variance is the limit variance of the bias-corrected exact
estimator.

\textbf{Proof.} By Lemma 8.2,
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]
The heterogeneity term is asymptotically normal by Lemma 8.6.

For the residual term, apply the shared conditional triangular-array CLT
lemma to the array \[\xi_{n,i}
:=
\frac{w(\mathbf X_i)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\qquad\text{for }Z_i=1,\] and \[\xi_{n,j}
:=
-\frac{K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\qquad\text{for }Z_j=0.\] Lemma 8.3 gives the conditional mean-zero
property and the exact conditional variance formula. Closure Input 8.5
gives the variance limit and the Lyapunov condition. Therefore
\[\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).\] The covariance
between the heterogeneity term and the residual term is zero because the
residual block has conditional mean zero given the sigma-field generated
by the covariates, treatments, and matching graph, while the
heterogeneity block is measurable with respect to the same sigma-field.
Therefore the total limit variance is
\[V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\] The
bias-corrected conclusion follows by Slutsky's theorem. \(\square\)

\medskip
\textbf{Expanded chain-of-equation proof of Theorem 8.7.}

Write
\[
W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}:=\sqrt n\,H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\qquad
M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}:=\sqrt n\,E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Lemma~8.2 gives
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
=
W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}.
\]

\subsubsection{Step 1. The heterogeneity block for prospective PATT}\label{step-1.-the-heterogeneity-block-for-prospective-patt}

Lemma~8.6 proves
\[
W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}\right),
\]
and the block is \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\)-measurable.

\subsubsection{Step 2. The matched unit-residual block for prospective PATT}\label{step-2.-the-matched unit-residual-block-for-prospective-patt}

Define
\[
\xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}
:=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}}
\left[
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=1\}w(\mathbf X_j)\varepsilon_{1\mid1,j}
-
\mathbf 1\{Z_j=0\}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right].
\]
Then
\[
M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}=\sum_{j=1}^n \xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}.
\]
Conditional mean zero follows exactly as in the retrospective PATT proof immediately above:
\[
E\!\left[\xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]=0.
\]
Lemma~8.3 gives the exact predictable quadratic variation:
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}
&:=
\sum_{j=1}^n E\!\left[\left(\xi_{n,j}^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}\right)^2\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right]\\
&=
\frac{1}{n\bar m_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n}^2}
\left[
\sum_{j:Z_j=1}w(\mathbf X_j)^2\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X_j)
+
\sum_{j:Z_j=0}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2\sigma_0^2(\mathbf X_j)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Closure Input~8.5 states that
\[
Q_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}\to_p V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}},
\]
and that the Lyapunov remainder tends to zero. Hence Lemma~4.2 gives
\[
M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and Imported Lemma~4.2B upgrades the convergence to stable convergence
relative to \(\mathcal F_{n,0}\).

\subsubsection{Step 3. Combine the two prospective PATT blocks}\label{step-3.-combine-the-two-prospective-patt-blocks}

For any real \(t\),
\[
E\!\left[e^{it(W_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+M_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}})}\right]
=
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}}
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)\right].
\]
The stable martingale limit gives
\[
E\!\left(e^{itM_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}}\mid \mathcal F_{n,0}\right)
\to_p
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
while Lemma~8.6 gives
\[
E\!\left[e^{itW_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}}\right]
\to
\exp\!\left(-\tfrac12 t^2V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}\right).
\]
Therefore
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]
The bias-corrected version follows by the same Slutsky calculation as in
the three preceding known-score proofs.
\(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 8.7A} (Prospective PATT variance written on the one-sided score scale).
Under the assumptions of Theorem~8.7,
\[V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  Zw(\mathbf X)
  \bigl\{
    \mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
  \bigr\}
\right)}{\left(E_s[Zw(\mathbf X)]\right)^2}.
\]
The one-sided prospective bias discrepancy may be written as
\[\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)
=
m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))-
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{P,(0),\mathrm{ex}}m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_j)).\]

\textbf{Proof.} Lemma~3.6A gives
\[\tau_T(\mathbf X)=\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\qquad\text{a.s.}\]
Substitute this identity into the definition of \(V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}\).
The bias identity follows from Lemma~3.6A with the prospective PATT
coefficients. \(\square\)

\section{8A. Known-score exact bias-corrected estimators in the four
cells}\label{a.-known-score-exact-bias-corrected-estimators-in-the-four-cells}

The centered known-score limits in Sections 5--8 show that the exact
mean-discrepancy term is of root-\(n\) order and therefore must either
be retained explicitly or removed by exact bias correction. This
subsection records the exact known-score bias-corrected estimators with
standard \(\mathrm{BC}\) notation.

\textbf{Definition 8A.1} (Known-score exact bias-corrected PATE imputation).
Fix \(z\in\{0,1\}\). For a focal unit \(i\) in arm \(1-z\), define
\[\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(z)
:=
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{(z),\mathrm{ex}}\{Y_j-\mu_z(\mathbf X_j)\}+\mu_z(\mathbf X_i).\]
For a sampled unit already in arm \(z\), define
\[\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(z):=Y_i.\] Then define
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)},\]
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)\{\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(1)-\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)}.\]

\textbf{Proposition 8A.2} (Known-score exact bias-corrected PATE
decomposition). Under the known-score PATE score \(\Psi_z(\mathbf X)=\mu_z(\mathbf X)\),
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
=
H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}},\]
and
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
=
H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} For a treated focal unit \(i\), \[\begin{aligned}
Y_i-\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)
&=
Y_i-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\{Y_j-\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\}-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\tau(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] For a control focal unit \(i\), \[\begin{aligned}
\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(1)-Y_i
&=
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(1),\mathrm{ex}}\{Y_j-\mu_1(\mathbf X_j)\}+\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-Y_i\\
&=
\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}+\mu_1(\mathbf X_i)-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)-\varepsilon_{0,i}\\
&=
\tau(\mathbf X_i)+\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(1),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{1,j}-\varepsilon_{0,i}.
\end{aligned}\] Multiply by the relevant outer weights, sum, divide by
the relevant Hájek denominator, add and subtract the target, and reindex
the matched unit residual contributions matched unit-by-matched unit exactly as in Lemmas 5.1
and 7.1. No mean-discrepancy term remains because it has been removed
identically inside the imputed contrasts. \(\square\)

\textbf{Definition 8A.3} (Known-score exact bias-corrected PATT imputation).
For treated focal units \(i\) define
\[\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)
:=
\sum_{j=1}^n \lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\{Y_j-\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\}+\mu_0(\mathbf X_i),
\qquad Z_i=1,\] and let
\(\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(1):=Y_i\). Then define
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{Y_i-\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)},\]
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
:=
\frac{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w(\mathbf X_i)\{Y_i-\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)\}}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w(\mathbf X_i)}.\]

\textbf{Proposition 8A.4} (Known-score exact bias-corrected PATT
decomposition). Under the known-score one-sided PATT score
\(\Psi_0(\mathbf X)=\mu_0(\mathbf X)\),
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
H_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}},\]
and
\[\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
H_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}+E_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.\]

\textbf{Proof.} For every treated focal unit \(i\), \[\begin{aligned}
Y_i-\widehat Y_{i,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(0)
&=
Y_i-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\{Y_j-\mu_0(\mathbf X_j)\}-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}-\mu_0(\mathbf X_i)\\
&=
\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^n\lambda_{i\to j}^{(0),\mathrm{ex}}\varepsilon_{0,j}.
\end{aligned}\] Multiply by the relevant treated outer weight, sum over
treated focal units, divide by the relevant treated Hájek denominator,
add and subtract the target, and reindex the matched unit residual contribution
matched unit-by-matched unit exactly as in Lemmas 6.1 and 8.1. Again there is no
mean-discrepancy term because the known-score correction removes it
identically. \(\square\)



\textbf{Corollary 8.7B} (Prospective PATT exact centered known-score CLT under the primitive sufficient route). Assume Assumptions~2.1, 2.3, and~2.5. Assume further that Proposition~4.8C holds on the one-sided control matched-arm score space \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\), and that Proposition~8.5A holds. Then all closure requirements of Theorem~8.7 are satisfied. Consequently,
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}-B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and if a bias-corrected exact estimator removes \(B_{n,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) at \(o_p(n^{-1/2})\) scale, then the same variance is the limiting variance of the bias-corrected exact estimator.

\textbf{Proof.} Proposition~4.8C gives Closure Input~4.7A on the one-sided control matched-arm score space, so Corollary~4.12 supplies the exact matching-frequency moment limits used in Section~8. Proposition~8.5A delivers Closure Input~8.5. Lemma~8.6 then gives the heterogeneity CLT and Theorem~8.7 gives the centered known-score exact CLT. \(\square\)


\subsection{8.5 Estimated-score prospective PATT results}\label{prospective-patt-estimated-score-results}

For the prospective PATT cell, let
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
:=
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Let \(\widehat\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}\) collect the prospective propensity-score estimator together with the one-sided control prognostic-score estimator, and let \(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d}\) denote its midpoint discretization on cubes of side length \(d/\sqrt n\).

\textbf{Theorem 8.8} (Prospective PATT estimated-score limits, fixed law and moving target). Assume Theorem~8.7, Section~10.1B, and Closure Input~10.1 specialized to the prospective PATT cell.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item \textbf{Fixed-law scientific target.}
Under Proposition~10.3,
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
&=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
-\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
By the discretization transfer in Imported Lemma~10.1B, the same limit holds with \(\widehat\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}\) in place of \(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d}\).

\item \textbf{Score-indexed moving-target extension.}
Under Proposition~10.5A specialized to the prospective PATT cell,
\[
\tau_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
=
\frac{
E_{s,\theta}\!\left[
Zw(\mathbf X)
\left\{
\mu_{1\mid1,\theta}(\mathbf X)
-
m_{s,0,\theta}(\mathbf D_{0,\theta}(\mathbf X))
\right\}
\right]
}{
E_{s,\theta}[Zw(\mathbf X)]
},
\]
and
\[
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}
:=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
\tau_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
\right|_{\theta=\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^\ast}.
\]
Then
\[
\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
P\!\left[
\sqrt n\,
\bigl\{V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}\bigr\}^{-1/2}
\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
\right\}
\le z
\right]
=
\Phi(z),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
&=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
+
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}\\
&=
\left\{
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
\right\}
-
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}
+
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\sqrt n\left\{
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
-
\tau_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{red}}(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d})
\right\}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}\right).
\]
If the stronger full-ignorability shortcut of Corollary~10.5 is imposed, then \(\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}\) may be replaced by the corresponding explicit quotient derivative.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Proof.} Theorem~8.7 gives
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
=
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}+V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}.
\]
Apply Theorem~10.6 with the cell label \(A=(P,\mathrm{PATT})\). Proposition~10.3 yields the fixed-law result. Proposition~10.5A yields the prospective one-sided score-indexed moving-target derivative and hence the additional block
\[
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}^\top
\Sigma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},\theta}
\gamma_{P,\mathrm{PATT},2}.
\]
Imported Lemma~10.1B transfers the result from \(\bar\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT},n,d}\) to \(\widehat\theta_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 8.8A} (Prospective PATT keeps the one-sided target interpretation). The cell-specific feasible-score formulas above are written on the one-sided PATT route throughout. In particular, the treated-side heterogeneity block uses \(\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X)\), \(\tau_T(\mathbf X)\), and \(\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X)\), not the stronger two-sided objects \(\mu_1(\mathbf X)\), \(\tau(\mathbf X)\), and \(\sigma_1^2(\mathbf X)\), unless the stronger shortcut is imposed separately.

\subsection{8.6 Linearization-based bootstrap for prospective PATT}\label{prospective-patt-bootstrap-main-text}

For each bootstrap replicate \(b=1,\ldots,B\), draw
\[
(m_1^{\ast,b},\ldots,m_n^{\ast,b})
\sim
\mathrm{Multinomial}(n;1/n,\ldots,1/n),
\]
and define
\[
w_i^{\ast,b}:=m_i^{\ast,b}w(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Refit the prospective propensity-score estimator and the control-side prognostic-score estimator exactly as in Appendix~O.4, and let \(\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}\) denote the replicated bias-correction regression on the replicated control score space.

Define the prospective PATT linear term
\[
\varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATT},i}
=
\begin{cases}
w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\}, & Z_i=1,\\[1mm]
-K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(i)\varepsilon_{0,i}, & Z_i=0.
\end{cases}
\]
The prospective PATT bootstrap replicate is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\ast,b}
=
\frac{
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}
\{Y_i-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))\}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0} m_j^{\ast,b}K_{n,0}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(j)
\{Y_j-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_j))\}
}{
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}
}.
\]

\textbf{Proposition 8.9} (Prospective PATT bootstrap linearization and variance target). Under the conditions of Theorem~8.8 and the bootstrap first-step replication conditions recorded in Appendix~O.4,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}}
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}
\sum_{i=1}^n
(m_i^{\ast,b}-1)
\left\{
\varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATT},i}
-
\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}Z_iw(\mathbf X_i)
\right\}
+
o_{p^\ast}(1),
\]
where
\[
\widehat N_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}
:=
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w(\mathbf X_i).
\]
Consequently, conditionally on the original sample,
\[
\sqrt n\left(
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\ast,b}
-
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC,ex}}
\right)
\Rightarrow_p
N\!\left(0,V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}\right),
\]
with
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{known\text{-}score,ex}}
\]
under the known-score theory,
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{fix,ex}}
\]
under the fixed-law estimated-score theory, and
\[
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{boot}}
=
V_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{chg,ex}}
\]
under the moving-target estimated-score extension.

\textbf{Proof.} This is Proposition~O.1 specialized to the prospective PATT cell. Change only the common treated-side H\'ajek weight, the one-sided prospective control matching-frequency term, and the replicated one-sided score estimator. Appendix~O.4--O.5 then yields the displayed bootstrap linearization and the three variance targets. \(\square\)


\section{9. Boundary between the closed-path theorems and the remaining frontier closure pieces}\label{a-direct-honesty-section-what-the-exact-theory-proves-and-what-it-does-not-prove}

The current exact four-cell theory proves the following.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  The exact estimator algebra is correct in all four cells.
\item
  The exact reuse load is the reuse object generated by the exact
  estimator. It is not the equal-share proxy reuse factor.
\item
  The exact matching-frequency rewrite is correct in all four cells.
\item
  The exact decomposition into heterogeneity, matching discrepancy, and
  residual terms is correct in all four cells.
\item
  The exact conditional residual variance is correct in all four cells.
\item
  The exact centered known-score CLTs are correct in all four cells under
  explicit \textbf{exact survey-weighted matching-frequency stabilization assumptions} and
  explicit heterogeneity CLTs.
\item
  The exact bias is generically of root-\(n\) order in the
  two-dimensional score problem and therefore must either be retained in
  the centered expansion or removed by bias correction.
\end{enumerate}

The current exact four-cell theory does \textbf{not} prove the following
from first principles.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  It does not prove a completely comparator-free stochastic-geometry
  derivation of the exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moment theorem. The exact moment
  theorem in Section 4.4 is proved for the exact survey-weighted matching frequency, but it does
  so through the exact denominator identity, the unchanged
  two-dimensional equal-share catchment theorem, and explicit exact
  remainder conditions together with Proposition 4.8A.
\item
  It does not prove that the exact survey-weighted matching-frequency stabilization assumptions
  follow from lower-level geometric conditions alone.
\item
  It does not prove a survey-weighted balancing-score / multiple-robust
  identification theorem on reduced score space.
\item
  It does not prove a primitive weighted non-smooth perturbation theorem
  for estimated scores.
\item
  It does not prove full clustered, stratified, or multi-stage
  design-based survey asymptotics.
\end{enumerate}

This separation is deliberate. The equal-share comparator never
appears as the theorem object of the estimator. It appears only as an
unchanged internal geometric input inside the proof of the exact
survey-weighted matching-frequency theorem in Section 4.4.

\section{10. Shared non-smooth estimated-score proof infrastructure for the four scenario theorems}\label{estimated-score-extensions-fixed-law-and-changing-law}

Sections~5--8 now state the complete cell-specific estimated-score and
bootstrap results alongside the corresponding known-score results. The
present section supplies the common non-smooth local-experiment proof
infrastructure used by those four scenario-specific theorems. It is
deliberately conditional: it records the correct fixed-law and
changing-law target maps and the correct second adjustment block, but it
does not claim to prove the required weighted non-smooth perturbation
theorem from primitive matching geometry.

\textbf{Remark 10.0A} (Where martingale logic ends and Le Cam/LAN logic
begins). The known-score four-cell theory above already uses an explicit
conditional triangular-array / martingale step to close the centered
residual-array limits. The estimated-score problem is different: it
requires a local perturbation analysis of the non-smooth matching map
when the score is replaced by an estimated score. That is the place
where a survey-weighted analogue of the Abadie--Imbens / Yang--Zhang
local-experiment logic would live. This note records the resulting
fixed-law and changing-law variance formulas conditionally, but it does
not claim that the required weighted non-smooth LAN perturbation theorem
has already been proved from first principles.

\subsection{10.1 Cell-specific score
parameters}\label{cell-specific-score-parameters}

For each cell
\(A\in\{R\text{-PATE},R\text{-PATT},P\text{-PATE},P\text{-PATT}\}\) let
\[\theta_A\] collect the propensity-score parameters and the
prognostic-score parameters used in the matching score.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  In retrospective PATE, \(\theta_A\) indexes \(e(X;\theta_A)\),
  \(\Psi_1(X;\theta_A)\), and \(\Psi_0(X;\theta_A)\).
\item
  In retrospective PATT, \(\theta_A\) indexes \(e(X;\theta_A)\) and
  \(\Psi_0(X;\theta_A)\).
\item
  In prospective PATE, \(\theta_A\) indexes \(e(X;\theta_A)\),
  \(\Psi_1(X;\theta_A)\), and \(\Psi_0(X;\theta_A)\).
\item
  In prospective PATT, \(\theta_A\) indexes \(e(X;\theta_A)\) and
  \(\Psi_0(X;\theta_A)\).
\end{itemize}

Let \(\widehat\theta_A\) denote the corresponding score estimator.

\subsection{10.1A Imported Le Cam and discretization machinery used only
in the estimated-score
layer}\label{a-imported-le-cam-and-discretization-machinery-used-only-in-the-estimated-score-layer}

The known-score four-cell theory in Sections 4--9 is based on exact
matching-frequency algebra, exact survey-weighted matching-frequency moment limits, and the
martingale-array CLT already proved above. The estimated-score layer uses
two additional probability blocks that are logically separate from the
known-score layer:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  the generic Le Cam local-substitution machinery that translates the
  local experiment into a Gaussian mean shift; and
\item
  the Andreou--Werker discretization transfer that moves the argument
  from a deterministic local shift \(h\) to a discretized feasible
  score.
\end{enumerate}

We state both imported ingredients explicitly so that the dependence map
is explicit and complete.

\textbf{Imported Lemma 10.1A} (Le Cam's third lemma; imported
unchanged). Let \[Z_n\] be any statistic such that, under a local
experiment \(P^{\theta_n}\), \[\left(
Z_n,
\log\frac{dP^{\theta^\ast}}{dP^{\theta_n}}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\begin{bmatrix}
0\\[2pt]-\tfrac12 h^\top I h
\end{bmatrix},
\begin{bmatrix}
\Sigma_Z & C h\\[2pt]
h^\top C^\top & h^\top I h
\end{bmatrix}
\right).\] Then under \(P^{\theta^\ast}\),
\[Z_n\Rightarrow N(C h,\Sigma_Z).\]

\textbf{Proof.} This is Le Cam's third lemma in its standard locally
asymptotically normal form; see Le Cam and Yang (1990), van der Vaart
(2000), Andreou and Werker (2012), Abadie and Imbens (2016), and Yang
and Zhang (2022). The statement is imported unchanged. \(\square\)

\textbf{Imported Lemma 10.1B} (Andreou--Werker discretization transfer;
only its role is imported unchanged). Let \(S_n(h)\) be a statistic
indexed by \(h\in\mathbb R^q\). Let \(\widehat h_n\) be an estimator and
let \(\bar h_{n,d}\) be the midpoint discretization of \(\widehat h_n\)
on a grid of cubes with side length \(d/\sqrt n\). Assume:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  for every fixed \(h\), under the baseline law,
  \[S_n(h)\Rightarrow G(h);\]
\item
  for every fixed \(d>0\), \[\widehat h_n-\bar h_{n,d}=o_p(1);\]
\item
  for every fixed \(d>0\),
  \[S_n(\widehat h_n)-S_n(\bar h_{n,d})=o_p(1);\]
\item
  as \(n\to\infty\) and then \(d\downarrow0\),
  \[\bar h_{n,d}\Rightarrow H,\] and the limit family \(G(h)\) is
  continuous in \(h\) on compact sets in the sense required to pass from
  a deterministic local index to the random discretized index.
\end{enumerate}

Then, in the same double limit, \[S_n(\widehat h_n)\Rightarrow G(H).\]

\textbf{Proof.} This is the discretization / residual-statistic transfer
device used by Andreou and Werker (2012) and by Abadie and Imbens
(2016). In the present note we import only this transfer role: once the
deterministic-\(h\) Gaussian law has been proved, the discretized
estimated score may replace the deterministic local point without
changing the limit. \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 10.1C} (What is imported unchanged and what is
cell-specific). The two probability inputs above are imported only at
the level of their probabilistic role. What is specific to exact WDSM is
the verification of the local Gaussian block for the known-score exact
statistic under the selected-sample weighted law and the verification of
the discretization stability clause for the exact WDSM functional. Those
two tasks are the substantive weighted non-smooth frontier. Section~10.1B handles the ordinary smooth survey-weighted M-estimation part of the first step; Closure Input 10.1 adds only the local score experiment, the joint Gaussian coupling with the known-score exact statistic, and the discretization transfer. Those extra pieces are then used in Proposition 10.2
and Theorem 10.6 below.


\subsection{10.1B Primitive weighted M-estimation block for the first-step score estimators}\label{primitive-weighted-m-estimation-block-in-section-10}

This subsection isolates the part of the estimated-score layer that is ordinary survey-weighted estimating-equation theory rather than matching-specific non-smooth analysis. It verifies the basic sandwich central limit theorem and the asymptotic linearization used by the cell-specific score estimators. What it does \emph{not} verify is the local likelihood-ratio embedding, the joint Gaussian coupling with the non-smooth known-score matching statistic, or the Andreou--Werker discretization step; those ingredients remain in Section~10.

\textbf{Auxiliary Regularity Block M.E1} (Ordinary weighted M-estimation
regularity for a fixed cell). Fix a cell \(A\), let
\(U_A(O;\theta)\in\mathbb R^{p_A}\) denote the stacked estimating
function actually used in that cell, and define the sample equation
\[
\mathbb U_{A,n}(\theta):=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\theta).
\]
Assume the following.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \(\theta_A^\ast\in \operatorname{int}(\Theta_A)\) and
  \(\widehat\theta_A\to_p\theta_A^\ast\).
\item
  \(E[U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)]=0\), and there exists \(\delta>0\) and a
  neighborhood \(\mathcal N_A\) of \(\theta_A^\ast\) such that
  \[
  \sup_{\theta\in\mathcal N_A}
  E\!\left[\|U_A(O;\theta)\|^{2+\delta}\right]
  <\infty.
  \]
\item
  \(U_A(O;\theta)\) is continuously differentiable in \(\theta\) on
  \(\mathcal N_A\), and
  \[
  \Gamma_A
  :=
  E\!\left[
    \frac{\partial U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)}{\partial\theta^\top}
  \right]
  \]
  is nonsingular.
\item
  The estimator approximately solves the sample equation:
  \[
  \mathbb U_{A,n}(\widehat\theta_A)=o_p(n^{-1/2}).
  \]
\end{enumerate}

Define
\[
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)
:=
-\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\theta_A^\ast),
\]
\[
\Sigma_{A,U}
:=
E\!\left[
U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)^\top
\right],
\]
and
\[
\Sigma_{A,\theta}
:=
\Gamma_A^{-1}\Sigma_{A,U}\Gamma_A^{-T}.
\]

\textbf{Lemma M.1} (Ordinary weighted first-step CLT and asymptotic
linearity). Under Auxiliary Regularity Block~M.E1,
\[
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)\Rightarrow N(0,\Sigma_{A,U}),
\]
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
=
\Gamma_A^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)+o_p(1),
\]
and therefore
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)\Rightarrow N(0,\Sigma_{A,\theta}).
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Start from the approximate sample estimating equation and
change one object at a time:
\[
\mathbb U_{A,n}(\widehat\theta_A)
=
o_p(n^{-1/2})
\]
\[
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\widehat\theta_A)
=
o_p(n^{-1/2}).
\]
Apply the mean-value expansion coordinatewise between
\(\widehat\theta_A\) and \(\theta_A^\ast\):
\[
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\widehat\theta_A)
=
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\theta_A^\ast)
+
\left\{
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
\frac{\partial U_A(O_i;\widetilde\theta_A)}{\partial\theta^\top}
\right\}
(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast),
\]
where \(\widetilde\theta_A\) lies on the line segment joining
\(\widehat\theta_A\) and \(\theta_A^\ast\). Multiply the last display by
\(\sqrt n\):
\[
\sqrt n\,\mathbb U_{A,n}(\widehat\theta_A)
=
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\theta_A^\ast)
+
\left\{
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
\frac{\partial U_A(O_i;\widetilde\theta_A)}{\partial\theta^\top}
\right\}
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast).
\]
Now move the first summand to the other side and rewrite it using the
definition of \(\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)\):
\[
-\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\theta_A^\ast)
=
\left\{
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
\frac{\partial U_A(O_i;\widetilde\theta_A)}{\partial\theta^\top}
\right\}
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
+o_p(1)
\]
\[
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)
=
\left\{
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
\frac{\partial U_A(O_i;\widetilde\theta_A)}{\partial\theta^\top}
\right\}
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
+o_p(1).
\]
Because \(\widehat\theta_A\to_p\theta_A^\ast\), the intermediate point
\(\widetilde\theta_A\to_p\theta_A^\ast\). Therefore the sample Jacobian
converges in probability to \(\Gamma_A\):
\[
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
\frac{\partial U_A(O_i;\widetilde\theta_A)}{\partial\theta^\top}
=
\Gamma_A+o_p(1).
\]
Substitute this into the previous display:
\[
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)
=
\left\{\Gamma_A+o_p(1)\right\}
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
+o_p(1).
\]
Rearrange one factor at a time:
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
=
\left\{\Gamma_A+o_p(1)\right\}^{-1}
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)
+o_p(1)
\]
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
=
\Gamma_A^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)+o_p(1).
\]
This proves the asymptotic linearity display.

For the first display, the summands
\(-U_A(O_i;\theta_A^\ast)\) are independent mean-zero vectors under the selected-sample i.i.d. superpopulation assumption with finite
\((2+\delta)\)-th moments, so the multivariate Lindeberg--Feller central
limit theorem gives
\[
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)\Rightarrow N(0,\Sigma_{A,U}).
\]
Apply Slutsky's theorem to the linearization above:
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
=
\Gamma_A^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast)+o_p(1)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
0,\Gamma_A^{-1}\Sigma_{A,U}\Gamma_A^{-T}
\right).
\]
By definition of \(\Sigma_{A,\theta}\), this is exactly
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)\Rightarrow N(0,\Sigma_{A,\theta}).
\]
This completes the proof. \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark M.1A} (What the lemma removes from the main-text
assumption burden). Lemma~M.1 shows that the \emph{ordinary first-step}
part of the estimated-score layer is not a matching-specific theorem
frontier. What remains genuinely additional after this reduction is:
\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  the local likelihood-ratio / local-experiment block for the nuisance
  law, unless one is willing to impose a standard regular LAN model for
  that first step;
\item
  the known-score exact-WDSM local martingale representation under local
  paths;
\item
  the joint local Gaussian block linking the known-score statistic to the
  nuisance score; and
\item
  the Andreou--Werker discretization / local-substitution verification
  for the exact survey-weighted WDSM plug-in statistic.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Remark M.1B} (How Section~10.1B and Section~10 now fit together).
Section~10.1B remains on the generic weighted estimating-equation scale
because that is the correct level for ordinary consistency and sandwich
asymptotic linearity of the cell-specific score estimators. Section~10,
however, no longer combines that generic sandwich block directly with
the likelihood-ratio algebra. Instead Section~10 switches to the
score-normalized local-likelihood scale
\[
\Sigma_{A,\theta}=\mathcal I_A^{-1},
\]
which is exactly the scale used by Abadie--Imbens (2016) and
Yang--Zhang (2022) for the non-smooth Le Cam argument. Thus the generic
sandwich lemma in Section~10.1B is preparatory, while the actual
estimated-score theorem is deliberately stated on the local-score scale.




\subsection{10.2 Local semiparametric experiment and nuisance-score
notation}\label{local-semiparametric-experiment-and-nuisance-score-notation}

Fix a cell \(A\). For a fixed local direction \(h\), define the local
path
\[
\theta_{A,n}=\theta_A^\ast+\frac{h}{\sqrt n}.
\]
Let \(P_A^{\theta_{A,n}}\) denote the corresponding local law. In the
estimated-score layer we now work directly on the \emph{score-normalized
local-likelihood scale} used in Abadie--Imbens (2016) and Yang--Zhang
(2022), rather than mixing a generic weighted M-estimation Jacobian with
the local likelihood ratio.

Let \(\ell_A(O;\theta)\) denote the cell-specific log-likelihood or
survey-weighted pseudo-log-likelihood contribution used for the
first-step score estimator in cell \(A\). Define the local central score
process
\[
\mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta)
:=
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n
\frac{\partial \ell_A(O_i;\theta)}{\partial\theta},
\]
and define the corresponding information matrix
\[
\mathcal I_A
:=
E\!\left[
-
\frac{\partial^2 \ell_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta^\top}
\right]
=
E\!\left[
\frac{\partial \ell_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)}{\partial\theta}
\frac{\partial \ell_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)}{\partial\theta^\top}
\right].
\]
Write
\[
\Sigma_{A,\theta}:=\mathcal I_A^{-1}.
\]
Let
\[
\Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
\]
be the local log-likelihood ratio of \(P_A^{\theta_A^\ast}\) relative to
\(P_A^{\theta_{A,n}}\).

\textbf{Remark 10.2A} (Where survey weights enter the local score block).
In retrospective cells, the local score in \(\ell_A\) must encode the
survey-weighted propensity contribution appropriate for that cell:
either the population propensity score is estimated directly through a
survey-weighted pseudo-likelihood score, or the selected-sample
propensity score is embedded first and then mapped back to the
population propensity through the Bayes relation developed in
Section~3. In prospective cells, because the selected-sample and
population propensity scores coincide, the same notation covers the
ordinary unweighted propensity likelihood as a special case. The
prognostic score part of the first step may be weighted or unweighted
according to the cell-specific design logic in the main manuscript, but
the local experiment below is written only at the score-normalized LAN
level.

\textbf{Remark 10.2B} (Equivalent unnormalized nuisance-process representation and the transpose placement). Section~10.1B proves the ordinary survey-weighted first-step linearization on the unnormalized estimating-equation scale. If one writes
\[
\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta)
:=
-\frac{1}{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n U_A(O_i;\theta),
\qquad
\Gamma_A
:=
E\!\left[\frac{\partial U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)}{\partial\theta^\top}\right],
\qquad
\Sigma_{A,U}
:=
E\!\left[U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)U_A(O;\theta_A^\ast)^\top\right],
\]
and
\[
\Sigma_{A,\theta}
:=
\Gamma_A^{-1}\Sigma_{A,U}\Gamma_A^{-\top},
\]
then the ordinary first-step expansion is
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})
=
\Gamma_A^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1).
\]
Therefore the score-normalized LAN process used in the main theorem can be rewritten one place at a time as
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})
&=
\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1)\\
&=
\Gamma_A^\top\Sigma_{A,U}^{-1}\Gamma_A\Gamma_A^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1)\\
&=
\Gamma_A^\top\Sigma_{A,U}^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence the reverse likelihood ratio on the ordinary unnormalized scale is
\[
\Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
=
-h^\top\Gamma_A^\top\Sigma_{A,U}^{-1}\mathcal V_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})
-\frac12 h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h
+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1).
\]
This is the transpose placement that yields
\(\operatorname{Cov}(\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n}),\Lambda_{A,n})=-h\)
without imposing symmetry of \(\Gamma_A\). The main text stays on the score-normalized scale because that is the cleanest way to keep the LAN covariance algebra and the non-smooth matching argument synchronized.

\textbf{Remark 10.2C} (No generic sandwich-only shortcut is being used in the theorem path). Proposition~10.2 and Theorem~10.6 are not claimed under an ordinary sandwich linearization alone. Section~10.1B supplies the smooth survey-weighted first-step asymptotic linearity. The additional reverse-likelihood block and the covariance simplification to \(-h\) are claimed only after the score-normalized LAN embedding in Closure Input~10.1 is imposed. Thus the current theorem path does not silently identify a generic weighted \(Z\)-estimation problem with a likelihood-score local experiment.

\subsection{10.3 Score-normalized non-smooth local-experiment regularity
block for estimated-score WDSM}\label{score-normalized-non-smooth-local-experiment-regularity-block-for-estimated-score-wdsm}

This section now follows the exact \emph{type} of non-smooth first-step
logic used in Abadie--Imbens (2016) and Yang--Zhang (2022): local
asymptotic normality for the nuisance-score block, a joint local
Gaussian limit for the known-score statistic and the nuisance score, Le
Cam's third lemma, and the Andreou--Werker discretization device. The
note no longer combines a generic weighted estimating-equation Jacobian
with the local likelihood ratio, because that mixture is exactly where
the earlier sign and symmetry problems arose. Section~10.1B is retained as
a separate reduction for the ordinary weighted M-estimation part of the
first step, but the main local-experiment theorem is now stated on the
likelihood-score scale itself.

\textbf{Closure Input 10.1} (Score-normalized local experiment for
estimated-score exact WDSM; AI16/YZ analogue). Fix a cell \(A\). Impose
the following. Whenever a local path
\(\theta_{A,n}(h)=\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n\) is involved below, the
stated weak-convergence and quadratic-variation limits are required to
hold locally uniformly for \(h\) in compact subsets of the tangent
space.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Local nuisance-score behavior on the LAN score scale.} Under
  \(P_A^{\theta_{A,n}}\),
  \[
  \sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})
  =
  \Sigma_{A,\theta}\,\mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})
  +o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1),
  \]
  and
  \[
  \mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})
  \Rightarrow
  N(0,\mathcal I_A).
  \]
  The local log-likelihood ratio satisfies
  \[
  \Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
  =
  -h^\top \mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})
  -\frac12 h^\top \mathcal I_A h
  +o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1).
  \]
\item
  \textbf{Known-score exact-WDSM local decomposition.} The bias-corrected
  exact known-score estimator satisfies
  \[
  \sqrt n\bigl(
    \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_{A,n})
    -
    \tau_A(\theta_{A,n})
  \bigr)
  =
  D_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1),
  \]
  where
  \[
  D_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})=\sum_{k=1}^{m_n}\xi_{A,n,k}
  \]
  is a square-integrable martingale-difference array with respect to a
  filtration \((\mathcal F_{A,n,k})_{k=0}^{m_n}\).
\item
  \textbf{Joint martingale limit with the nuisance score.} For every
  fixed scalar \(c_0\) and every conformable vector \(c\), define
  \[
  T_{A,n}(c_0,c)
  :=
  c_0D_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n})
  +
  c^\top \mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n}).
  \]
  The process \(T_{A,n}(c_0,c)\) admits a martingale-difference-array
  representation
  \[
  (\eta_{A,n,k}(c_0,c),\mathcal F_{A,n,k})_{k=1}^{m_n}
  \]
  with
  \[
  T_{A,n}(c_0,c)
  =
  \sum_{k=1}^{m_n}\eta_{A,n,k}(c_0,c)+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1),
  \]
  and, for some \(\delta>0\),
  \[
  \sum_{k=1}^{m_n}
  E_{\theta_{A,n}}
  \!\left[
    \left|\eta_{A,n,k}(c_0,c)\right|^{2+\delta}
  \right]
  \to 0,
  \]
  and
  \[
  \sum_{k=1}^{m_n}
  E_{\theta_{A,n}}
  \!\left[
    \eta_{A,n,k}(c_0,c)^2
    \mid
    \mathcal F_{A,n,k-1}
  \right]
  \to
  c_0^2V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
  +
  2c_0 c^\top \gamma_{A,1}
  +
  c^\top \mathcal I_A c
  \]
  in probability, where \(V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}\) is the exact
  known-score WDSM variance and \(\gamma_{A,1}\) is the column vector
  governing the local covariance between the known-score statistic and
  the nuisance \emph{score} process.
\item
