  \textbf{Discretization step.} The Le Cam discretization device used in
  Andreou--Werker and Abadie--Imbens applies to the present cell, so the
  lattice-discretized plug-in score can replace \(\widehat\theta_A\)
  without changing the limiting law.
\end{enumerate}

This is the correct non-smooth route. It does \emph{not} assume a direct
estimated-score expansion of
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
\]
as if the exact matching map were smooth. Instead it assumes the local
Gaussian experiment that is genuinely needed for a survey-weighted
Abadie--Imbens / Yang--Zhang type theorem.

\textbf{Remark 10.1A} (Why the main theorem is now written on the
score-normalized likelihood scale). The earlier
\((\Gamma_A,\Sigma_{A,U},\Sigma_{A,\theta})\) bookkeeping mixed a
generic weighted estimating equation with a likelihood-ratio expansion.
That mixture is not automatically valid unless the nuisance estimating
equation is itself the local score and the associated information
identity is imposed. The present rewrite therefore states the main
non-smooth theorem only on the local score scale
\((\mathcal S_{A,n},\mathcal I_A,\Sigma_{A,\theta})\), which is exactly
the level at which the source-paper Le Cam arguments are valid.

\textbf{Remark 10.1B} (What Section~10.1B still contributes after this
rewrite). Section~10.1B still proves the ordinary survey-weighted
estimating-equation consistency and asymptotic linearity used by the
cell-specific propensity and prognostic score estimators. What Section~10
adds beyond Section~10.1B is the local likelihood-ratio embedding, the
joint Gaussian coupling with the non-smooth matching statistic, and the
Andreou--Werker discretization step. Thus Section~10.1B remains useful, but
it is no longer incorrectly fused with the likelihood-ratio algebra.

\subsection{10.4 Joint local Gaussian limit for the known-score exact WDSM,
nuisance estimator, and likelihood
ratio}\label{joint-local-gaussian-limit-for-the-known-score-exact-wdsm-nuisance-estimator-and-likelihood-ratio}

\textbf{Proposition 10.2} (Joint local Gaussian limit on the
score-normalized LAN scale). Under Closure Input~10.1, under
\(P_A^{\theta_{A,n}}\),
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_{A,n})
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_{A,n})
\bigr)\\
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})\\
\Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
\end{array}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
0\\
0\\
-\frac12 h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h
\end{array}
\right),
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta} & -\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta} & -h\\
-h^\top\gamma_{A,1} & -h^\top & h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Write
\[
X_{A,n}
:=
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_{A,n})
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_{A,n})
\bigr),
\qquad
Y_{A,n}
:=
\mathcal S_{A,n}(\theta_{A,n}).
\]
By Closure Input~10.1(2) and 10.1(3), for every fixed \(c_0\) and
\(c\), the linear combination
\[
c_0X_{A,n}+c^\top Y_{A,n}
=
T_{A,n}(c_0,c)+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1)
\]
converges to a centered Normal law with variance
\[
c_0^2V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
+
2c_0c^\top \gamma_{A,1}
+
c^\top \mathcal I_A c.
\]
Therefore the Cramér--Wold device gives the joint limit
\[
\binom{X_{A,n}}{Y_{A,n}}
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\binom{0}{0},
\left(
\begin{array}{cc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\\
\gamma_{A,1} & \mathcal I_A
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]

Now use Closure Input~10.1(1) one term at a time. First,
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})
=
\Sigma_{A,\theta}Y_{A,n}+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1).
\]
Second,
\[
\Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
=
-h^\top Y_{A,n}
-\frac12 h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h
+o_{P_{\theta_{A,n}}}(1).
\]
The covariance blocks now follow by changing one object at a time:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(
  X_{A,n},
  \sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})
\bigr)
&=
\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(
  X_{A,n},
  \Sigma_{A,\theta}Y_{A,n}
\bigr)+o(1)\\
&=
E\bigl[X_{A,n}Y_{A,n}^\top\bigr]\Sigma_{A,\theta}+o(1)\\
&=
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}+o(1),
\end{aligned}
\]
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(
  X_{A,n},
  \Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
\bigr)
&=
\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(
  X_{A,n},
  -h^\top Y_{A,n}
\bigr)+o(1)\\
&=
-h^\top E\bigl[Y_{A,n}X_{A,n}\bigr]+o(1)\\
&=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h+o(1),
\end{aligned}
\]
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\bigl(
  \sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n})
\bigr)
&=
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\mathcal I_A\Sigma_{A,\theta}+o(1)\\
&=
\Sigma_{A,\theta}+o(1),
\end{aligned}
\]
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(
  \sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n}),
  \Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
\bigr)
&=
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\operatorname{Cov}(Y_{A,n},-h^\top Y_{A,n})+o(1)\\
&=
-\Sigma_{A,\theta}\mathcal I_A h+o(1)\\
&=
-h+o(1),
\end{aligned}
\]
and
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\bigl(
  \Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n})
\bigr)
&=
h^\top \mathcal I_A h+o(1)\\
&=
h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h+o(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Combining these blocks with the mean of the local likelihood ratio gives
the displayed Gaussian limit. \(\square\)

\subsection{10.5 Fixed-law target invariance in all four
cells}\label{fixed-law-target-invariance-in-all-four-cells}

\textbf{Proposition 10.3} (Fixed-law target invariance for the
scientific population target). Under the fixed-law interpretation, the
scientific target derivative is zero in all four cells:
\[
\gamma_{R\text{-PATE},2}=0,
\qquad
\gamma_{P\text{-PATE},2}=0,
\qquad
\gamma_{R\text{-PATT},2}=0,
\qquad
\gamma_{P\text{-PATT},2}=0.
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Under fixed law the superpopulation law of
\[
(Y(0),Y(1),\mathbf X,Z,S)
\]
does not change. Only the analyst's score map changes. Therefore the
scientific population targets remain constant.

For PATE,
\[
\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}(\theta_A)
=
E_{\Omega}[Y(1)-Y(0)]
=
\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}},
\]
so
\[
\frac{\partial\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}(\theta_A)}{\partial\theta_A}=0.
\]
The same argument holds under retrospective and prospective sampling,
because the population PATE is the same object in both cells.

For PATT,
\[
\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}(\theta_A)
=
E_{\Omega}[Y(1)-Y(0)\mid Z=1]
=
\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}},
\]
so
\[
\frac{\partial\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}(\theta_A)}{\partial\theta_A}=0.
\]
Again this holds in both sampling designs because the population treated
effect is unchanged. Therefore all four fixed-law target-derivative
vectors are zero. \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 10.3A} (Why Proposition~10.3 does not contradict the
moving-target extension below). Proposition~10.3 concerns the
\emph{scientific population target}. Proposition~10.5A below concerns the
\emph{score-indexed reduced target map} that appears in the Yang--Zhang
style local perturbation argument. Under fixed law, the scientific
target derivative is zero. Under a genuine moving-target local
experiment, the reduced target map may still have a nonzero derivative.
The two statements concern different objects.

\subsection{10.6 Genuine changing-law target
maps}\label{genuine-changing-law-target-maps}

The next proposition records the cleanest first-principles target maps
under the specific local experiment that changes the true treatment law
while holding the covariate law and potential-outcome laws fixed.

\textbf{Remark 10.4A} (Why the present main theorem path skips the toy treatment-law-only experiment). Earlier working drafts carried a separate treatment-law-only local experiment to generate explicit derivative formulas for a simplified changing-law route. Because that device can blur the distinction between the scientific fixed-law target and the Yang--Zhang-style reduced-score moving-target map, the present main-text theorem path does not use it. The operative moving-target object for Theorem~10.6 and for the four scenario-specific estimated-score theorems is Proposition~10.5A below.

\textbf{Proposition 10.5A} (Generic reduced-score target map for the second adjustment block; source-paper-aligned extension).
The structural population estimands \(\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\) and
\(\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\) remain the primary causal targets of this note.
For the non-smooth estimated-score argument, however, the generic
Yang--Zhang style moving-target object is the \emph{score-indexed target
map} \(\tau_A^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)\), not the fixed-law scientific
target from Proposition~10.3.

For each cell
\[
A\in\{R\text{-PATE},P\text{-PATE},R\text{-PATT},P\text{-PATT}\},
\]
define
\[
\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z):=
\begin{cases}
 w_Z(\mathbf X), & A=R\text{-PATE},\\
 w(\mathbf X), & A=P\text{-PATE},\\
 Zw_1(\mathbf X), & A=R\text{-PATT},\\
 Zw(\mathbf X), & A=P\text{-PATT}.
\end{cases}
\]
Define the score-indexed contrast \(\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)\) by
\[
\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X):=
\begin{cases}
 m_{s,1,\theta}(\mathbf D_{1,\theta}(\mathbf X))-m_{s,0,\theta}(\mathbf D_{0,\theta}(\mathbf X)),
 & A\in\{R\text{-PATE},P\text{-PATE}\},\\[4pt]
 \mu_{1\mid1,\theta}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0,\theta}(\mathbf D_{0,\theta}(\mathbf X)),
 & A\in\{R\text{-PATT},P\text{-PATT}\}.
\end{cases}
\]
Here
\[
m_{s,z,\theta}(d)
:=
E_{s,\theta}[Y\mid \mathbf D_{z,\theta}(\mathbf X)=d,Z=z].
\]
The reduced-score target map is
\[
\tau_A^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
:=
\frac{
E_{s,\theta}[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)]
}{
E_{s,\theta}[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)]
}.
\]
The derivative entering the moving-target extension is therefore
\[
\gamma_{A,2}
:=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
\tau_A^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
\right|_{\theta=\theta_A^\ast}.
\]
If the local experiment keeps the survey weights \(\omega_A\) fixed and
\(\theta\mapsto \eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)\) is differentiable at
\(\theta_A^\ast\), then
\[
\begin{aligned}
\gamma_{A,2}
&=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
\left\{
\frac{
E_s[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)]
}{
E_s[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)]
}
\right\}
\right|_{\theta=\theta_A^\ast}\\
&=
\frac{
E_s[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)\dot\eta_A(\mathbf X)]
}{
E_s[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)]
},
\end{aligned}
\]
where
\[
\dot\eta_A(\mathbf X)
:=
\left.
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)
\right|_{\theta=\theta_A^\ast}.
\]
In particular, the second adjustment block
\[
\gamma_{A,2}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}
\]
can be nonzero in both PATE and PATT cells.

\textbf{Proof.} Write
\[
N_A(\theta)
:=
E_{s,\theta}[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)],
\qquad
D_A(\theta)
:=
E_{s,\theta}[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)].
\]
Then
\[
\tau_A^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
=
\frac{N_A(\theta)}{D_A(\theta)}.
\]
Differentiate the quotient one object at a time:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\tau_A^{\mathrm{red}}(\theta)
&=
\frac{
\dot N_A(\theta)D_A(\theta)-N_A(\theta)\dot D_A(\theta)
}{
D_A(\theta)^2
}.
\end{aligned}
\]
If \(\omega_A\) is fixed along the local experiment, then
\(\dot D_A(\theta)=0\) and
\[
\begin{aligned}
\dot N_A(\theta)
&=
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
E_s[\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)]\\
&=
E_s\!\left[
\omega_A(\mathbf X,Z)
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\eta_{A,\theta}(\mathbf X)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Evaluate at \(\theta=\theta_A^\ast\) to obtain the displayed formula for
\(\gamma_{A,2}\). The last statement is immediate: whenever
\(\dot\eta_A\neq 0\) on a set of positive probability,
\(\gamma_{A,2}\) need not vanish, regardless of whether the cell is PATE
or PATT. \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 10.5A} (Fixed-law main theorem versus moving-target
extension). Proposition~10.3 remains the main-manuscript default for the
scientific fixed-law target. Proposition~10.5A is the separate
score-indexed moving-target extension used to align the note with
Yang--Zhang style local perturbations. The present theorem stack keeps
those two interpretations separate rather than silently treating them as
the same object.

\subsection{10.7 Feasible-score exact WDSM via Le Cam's third lemma and
discretization}\label{estimated-score-exact-wdsm-via-le-cams-third-lemma-and-discretization}

For exact WDSM, the correct estimated-score route is the same type of
route used in Abadie--Imbens and Yang--Zhang: first a joint local limit
for the known-score statistic, nuisance estimator, and likelihood ratio;
second Le Cam's third lemma; third the discretization step that turns a
deterministic local-score statement into a estimated-score statement.

The cleanest bookkeeping is to keep the \textbf{moving-target-centered}
statistic and the \textbf{fixed-target} recentering separate. That
separation is also the place where the sign of the changing-law
derivative becomes transparent. Section~10.1B now contains the ordinary
first-step weighted M-estimation proof, and Section~10.8 records the full
expanded local-shift algebra that earlier working versions had moved to a
separate appendix.

\textbf{Theorem 10.6} (Conditional estimated-score variance targets for
exact WDSM by the non-smooth LAN / Le Cam route). Under Closure
Condition~10.1, Proposition~10.2, Proposition~10.3, and
Proposition~10.5A, fix a cell \(A\) and let \(\bar\theta_{A,n,d}\)
denote the midpoint discretization of \(\widehat\theta_A\) on cubes of
side length \(d/\sqrt n\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  For every fixed local direction \(h\), under \(P_A^{\theta_A^\ast}\),
  the moving-target-centered pair obeys
  \[
  \left(
  \begin{array}{c}
  \sqrt n\bigl(
    \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
    -
    \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
  \bigr)\\
  \sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
  \end{array}
  \right)
  \Rightarrow
  N\!\left(
  \left(
  \begin{array}{c}
  -\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
  0
  \end{array}
  \right),
  \left(
  \begin{array}{cc}
  V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\\
  \Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta}
  \end{array}
  \right)
  \right).
  \]
  Consequently, if the limiting second coordinate is fixed at
  \(H_A=h\), then the first coordinate has conditional mean zero and
  conditional variance
  \[
  V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
  :=
  V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
  -
  \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}.
  \]
\item
  Under the fixed-law interpretation
  \(\tau_A(\theta)\equiv\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)\),
  \[
  \lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
  P\left(
    \sqrt n\,V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}^{-1/2}
    \bigl(
      \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
      -
      \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
    \bigr)
    \le z
  \right)
  =
  \Phi(z).
  \]
\item
  Under the score-indexed moving-target extension of
  Proposition~10.5A with derivative \(\gamma_{A,2}\),
  \[
  \lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
  P\left(
    \sqrt n\,V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}^{-1/2}
    \bigl(
      \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
      -
      \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
    \bigr)
    \le z
  \right)
  =
  \Phi(z),
  \]
  where
  \[
  V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}
  :=
  V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
  -
  \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}
  +
  \gamma_{A,2}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}.
  \]
  Equivalently, the moving-target-centered estimated-score statistic
  obeys
  \[
  \sqrt n\bigl(
    \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
    -
    \tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  \bigr)
  \Rightarrow
  N(0,V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}).
  \]
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Proof.} We proceed in the same three steps as the source-paper
proofs, now written on the score-normalized LAN scale.

\emph{Step 1: local Gaussian shift for the moving-target-centered
statistic.} Proposition~10.2 gives the joint local Gaussian limit under
\(P_A^{\theta_{A,n}}\). Imported Lemma~10.1A (Le Cam's third lemma) then
implies that under \(P_A^{\theta_A^\ast}\),
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
\bigr)\\
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast-h/\sqrt n)
\end{array}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
-h
\end{array}
\right),
\left(
\begin{array}{cc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\\
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta}
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]
Add \(h\) to the second coordinate one object at a time:
\[
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
=
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast-h/\sqrt n)+h.
\]
Therefore under \(P_A^{\theta_A^\ast}\),
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\\
Y_A
\end{array}
\right)
:=
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
\bigr)\\
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast)
\end{array}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
0
\end{array}
\right),
\left(
\begin{array}{cc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\\
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta}
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]

Now compute the conditional mean one object at a time. Condition on the
limiting second coordinate taking the value \(u\):
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A=u\}
&=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h
+
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}
\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}u\\
&=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h+\gamma_{A,1}^\top u.
\end{aligned}
\]
Set \(u=h\), which is exactly the local score value corresponding to the
direction \(h\). Then
\[
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A=h\}
=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h+\gamma_{A,1}^\top h
=
0.
\]
Likewise the conditional variance is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A\}
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}
\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}\\
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}\\
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}.
\end{aligned}
\]
This proves part~1.

\emph{Step 2: fixed-target recentering and the corrected sign of the
target derivative.} Change only the target centering:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
\bigr)\\
&\qquad+
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)-\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h+o(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Therefore, conditional on \(Y_A=h\), the fixed-target-centered statistic
has conditional mean
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\mid Y_A=h\}
&=
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A=h\}+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\\
&=
0+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\\
&=
\gamma_{A,2}^\top h.
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the correct sign. Under fixed law, Proposition~10.3 gives
\(\gamma_{A,2}=0\), so the conditional mean is zero. Under the moving
score-indexed extension of Proposition~10.5A, the additional mean shift
is \(\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\).

\emph{Step 3: discretization and estimated-score substitution.} Let
\[
\bar h_{A,n,d}:=\sqrt n(\bar\theta_{A,n,d}-\theta_A^\ast).
\]
Closure Input~10.1(4) gives the required approximation between
\(\widehat\theta_A\) and \(\bar\theta_{A,n,d}\), and Imported
Lemma~10.1B transfers the deterministic-\(h\) Gaussian law to the
discretized estimated score. Hence
\[
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  -
  \tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\bigr)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}\right).
\]
Under fixed law this already equals the fixed-target-centered statistic,
so part~2 follows.

Under the moving-target extension,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  -
  \tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\bigr)\\
&\qquad+
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})-\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  -
  \tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\bigr)
+
\gamma_{A,2}^\top\bar h_{A,n,d}
+
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term on the right-hand side has asymptotic conditional mean
zero given \(\bar h_{A,n,d}\). Therefore the asymptotic covariance
between the first term and \(\gamma_{A,2}^\top\bar h_{A,n,d}\) is zero.
The variance then adds one term at a time:
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
+
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\gamma_{A,2}^\top H_A\right)\\
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
+
\gamma_{A,2}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}\\
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}
+
\gamma_{A,2}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
This proves part~3. \(\square\)

\textbf{Corollary 10.6A} (Actual estimated-score estimator, not only its
lattice discretization). Under the conditions of Theorem~10.6, the same
fixed-law and moving-target limits hold with \(\widehat\theta_A\) in
place of \(\bar\theta_{A,n,d}\). In particular, under fixed law,
\[
\sqrt n\,V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}^{-1/2}
\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)
\Rightarrow
N(0,1),
\]
and under the moving-target extension,
\[
\sqrt n\,V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}^{-1/2}
\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)
\Rightarrow
N(0,1).
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Write the fixed-law statistic one object at a time:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&\qquad+
\sqrt n\Bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
-
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\Bigr).
\end{aligned}
\]
Closure Input~10.1(4) and Imported Lemma~10.1B imply that the second
term is \(o_p(1)\) after first letting \(n\to\infty\) and then
\(d\downarrow 0\). Therefore Slutsky's theorem transfers the fixed-law
limit from Theorem~10.6.

For the moving-target extension, change only the target centering:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\sqrt n\bigl(
  \widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
  -
  \tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&\qquad+
\sqrt n\Bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\widehat\theta_A)
-
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\Bigr),
\end{aligned}
\]
and the second term is again \(o_p(1)\). A second application of
Slutsky's theorem transfers the moving-target limit from
Theorem~10.6. \(\square\)

\subsection{10.8 Expanded local-shift algebra for Theorem 10.6}\label{expanded-local-shift-algebra-in-section-10}

This subsection rewrites the proof of Theorem~10.6 in companion
chain-of-equation form on the score-normalized LAN scale. Its purpose is
to make the sign bookkeeping explicit and to isolate the exact place
where the moving-target derivative enters.

\subsubsection{Cell-specific local
objects}\label{j.1-cell-specific-local-objects}

Fix one cell
\[
A\in\{R\text{-PATE},R\text{-PATT},P\text{-PATE},P\text{-PATT}\}.
\]
For a fixed vector \(h\), define the local path
\[
\theta_{A,n}(h):=\theta_A^\ast+\frac{h}{\sqrt n}.
\]
Define the moving-target-centered statistic
\[
X_{A,n}^\circ(h)
:=
\sqrt n\bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_{A,n}(h))
-
\tau_A(\theta_{A,n}(h))
\bigr),
\]
and the score-estimation term
\[
Y_{A,n}
:=
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_A^\ast).
\]

\subsubsection{Joint local law under the shifted
experiment}\label{j.2-joint-local-law-under-the-shifted-experiment}

By Proposition~10.2, under \(P_A^{\theta_{A,n}(h)}\),
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
X_{A,n}^\circ(h)\\
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n}(h))\\
\Lambda_{A,n}(\theta_A^\ast\mid\theta_{A,n}(h))
\end{array}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
0\\
0\\
-\tfrac12 h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h
\end{array}
\right),
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta} & -\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta} & -h\\
-h^\top\gamma_{A,1} & -h^\top & h^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}h
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]

\subsubsection{Le Cam shift back to the baseline
law}\label{j.3-le-cam-shift-back-to-the-baseline-law}

Imported Lemma~10.1A transfers the previous block from the local law to
the baseline law. Therefore, under \(P_A^{\theta_A^\ast}\),
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
X_{A,n}^\circ(h)\\
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n}(h))
\end{array}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
-h
\end{array}
\right),
\left(
\begin{array}{cc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\\
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta}
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]
Because
\[
Y_{A,n}
=
\sqrt n(\widehat\theta_A-\theta_{A,n}(h))+h,
\]
we also have
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
X_{A,n}^\circ(h)\\
Y_{A,n}
\end{array}
\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h\\
0
\end{array}
\right),
\left(
\begin{array}{cc}
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}} & \gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\\
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1} & \Sigma_{A,\theta}
\end{array}
\right)
\right).
\]

\subsubsection{Conditional Gaussian
algebra}\label{j.4-conditional-gaussian-algebra}

Condition on the limiting second coordinate taking the value \(u\).
Change one object at a time:
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A=u\}
&=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h
+
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}
\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}u\\
&=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h+\gamma_{A,1}^\top u.
\end{aligned}
\]
Set \(u=h\). Then
\[
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A=h\}
=
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top h+\gamma_{A,1}^\top h
=
0.
\]
Likewise,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Var}\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A\}
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}
\Sigma_{A,\theta}^{-1}
\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}\\
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{known-score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}.
\end{aligned}
\]

\subsubsection{Fixed-target recentering and the corrected
sign}\label{j.5-fixed-target-recentering-and-the-corrected-sign}

Now re-center at the fixed target:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)
-
\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)
+
\sqrt n\bigl(
\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast+h/\sqrt n)-\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h+o(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\mid Y_A=h\}
&=
E\{\mathbf X_A^\circ(h)\mid Y_A=h\}+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\\
&=
0+\gamma_{A,2}^\top h\\
&=
\gamma_{A,2}^\top h.
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the corrected sign.

\subsubsection{Why the moving-target variance block is
additive}\label{j.6-why-the-moving-target-variance-block-is-additive}

Let
\[
\bar h_{A,n,d}:=\sqrt n(\bar\theta_{A,n,d}-\theta_A^\ast).
\]
By Theorem~10.6,
\[
\sqrt n\bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
-
\tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\bigr)
\Rightarrow
N(0,V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}).
\]
Under the moving-target extension,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt n\bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
-
\tau_A(\theta_A^\ast)
\bigr)\\
&=
\sqrt n\bigl(
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
-
\tau_A(\bar\theta_{A,n,d})
\bigr)
+
\gamma_{A,2}^\top\bar h_{A,n,d}
+
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term has asymptotic conditional mean zero given
\(\bar h_{A,n,d}\), so the asymptotic covariance between the first term
and \(\gamma_{A,2}^\top\bar h_{A,n,d}\) is zero. Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
+
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\gamma_{A,2}^\top H_A\right)\\
&=
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
+
\gamma_{A,2}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
That is exactly the variance formula used in the main text.

\section{Appendix K. References used for imported
ingredients}\label{appendix-k.-references-used-for-imported-ingredients}

The present note imports results from and checks formulas against the following source
backbone.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Abadie, A. and Imbens, G. W. (2006). \emph{Large sample properties of
  matching estimators for average treatment effects.}
\item
  Abadie, A. and Imbens, G. W. (2012). \emph{A martingale representation
  for matching estimators.}
\item
  Abadie, A. and Imbens, G. W. (2016). \emph{Matching on the estimated
  propensity score.}
\item
  Andreou, E. and Werker, B. (2012). \emph{An alternative asymptotic
  analysis of residual-based statistics.}
\item
  Yang, S. and Zhang, P. (2022). \emph{Multiply robust matching
  estimators of average and quantile treatment effects.}
\item
  Chen, X. and Han, S. (2024). \emph{On the limiting variance of
  matching estimators.}
\item
  Hall, P. and Heyde, C. C. (1980). \emph{Martingale limit theory and
  its application.}
\item
  Le Cam, L. and Yang, G. L. (1990). \emph{Asymptotics in statistics:
  some basic concepts.}
\item
  van der Vaart, A. W. (2000). \emph{Asymptotic statistics.}
\end{enumerate}



\section{Appendix N. Main asymptotic formulas collected in one place}\label{appendix-n-main-asymptotic-formulas-collected-in-one-place}

This appendix collects the final probability limits and asymptotic
variance formulas without the surrounding derivations.

\textbf{Known-score centered limit in every cell.}
For each cell \(A\),
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{ex}}-\tau_A-B_{A,n}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,A}+V_{E,A}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right),
\]
and whenever a bias-corrected exact estimator satisfies
\[
\sqrt n\left(B_{A,n}^{\mathrm{ex}}-\widehat B_{A,n}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right)\to_p 0,
\]
we have
\[
\sqrt n\left(\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC,ex}}-\tau_A\right)
\Rightarrow
N\!\left(0,V_{H,A}+V_{E,A}^{\mathrm{ex}}\right).
\]

\textbf{Known-score residual variance on the score space.}
Write
\[
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(d)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-m_{s,z}(d)\mid \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)=d,\ Z=z
\right),
\]
\[
\nu_{P,\mathrm{PATE},z}(d)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-m_{s,z}(d)\mid \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)=d,\ Z=z
\right),
\]
\[
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{T}(d)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-m_{s,0}(d)\mid \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=d,\ Z=0
\right),
\qquad
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-\mu_{1\mid1}(\mathbf X)\mid \mathbf X,\ Z=1
\right),
\]
and analogously
\(\nu_{P,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{T}(d)\) and
\(\nu_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)\).
Then the known-score residual variance formulas are

\[
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{m_{R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{2}}
\sum_{z=0}^{1}
q_z\,
E_s\!\left[
  \nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))\,
  \chi_{z,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
  \,\middle|\, Z=z
\right],
\]

\[
V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{m_{P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{2}}
\sum_{z=0}^{1}
q_z\,
E_s\!\left[
  \nu_{P,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))\,
  \chi_{z,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
  \,\middle|\, Z=z
\right],
\]

\[
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{m_{R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{2}}
\left[
q_1\,E_s\!\left\{
w_1(\mathbf X)^2\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)\mid Z=1
\right\}
+
q_0\,E_s\!\left\{
  \nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{T}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\,
  \kappa_{0,2}^{R,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
  \mid Z=0
\right\}
\right],
\]

and
\[
V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{m_{P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{2}}
\left[
q_1\,E_s\!\left\{
w(\mathbf X)^2\nu_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)\mid Z=1
\right\}
+
q_0\,E_s\!\left\{
  \nu_{P,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{T}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))\,
  \kappa_{0,2}^{P,\mathrm{PATT},\mathrm{ex}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
  \mid Z=0
\right\}
\right].
\]

\textbf{Score-space heterogeneity rewritings.}
Under the mean-score specialization,
\[
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  w_Z(\mathbf X)\{m_{s,1}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}
\right)}{\{E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\}^2},
\]
\[
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  w(\mathbf X)\{m_{s,1}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X))-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}
\right)}{\{E_s[w(\mathbf X)]\}^2},
\]
\[
V_{H,R,\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  Z\,w_1(\mathbf X)\{\mu_{1\mid 1}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}
\right)}{\{E_s[Zw_1(\mathbf X)]\}^2},
\]
\[
V_{H,P,\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
  Z\,w(\mathbf X)\{\mu_{1\mid 1}(\mathbf X)-m_{s,0}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}\}
\right)}{\{E_s[Zw(\mathbf X)]\}^2}.
\]

\textbf{Estimated-score fixed-law limit.}
If the superpopulation law is fixed and only the analyst's score map is
estimated, then \(\gamma_{A,2}=0\) in all four cells and
\[
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
=
V_{A,\mathrm{or}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
-\gamma_{A,1}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}.
\]

\textbf{Estimated-score changing-law limit.}
Under a genuine local experiment in which the score-indexed target map
moves with \(\theta\),
\[
V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}
=
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
+\gamma_{A,2}^\top\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}.
\]

These are the final formulas used throughout the main text.




\section{Appendix O. Linearization-based bootstrap formulas for retrospective and prospective sampling in all four treatment-effect settings}\label{appendix-o-linearization-based-bootstrap-formulas-for-retrospective-and-prospective-sampling-in-all-four-treatment-effect-settings}

This appendix rewrites the linearization-based replication step in the notation of the main manuscript and records the four cell-specific bootstrap formulas explicitly. The guiding rule is the same in all four settings: the original nearest-neighbor match sets and the original survey-weighted matching frequencies are held fixed, while the linearized first-step and outcome components are perturbed by replication weights. This follows the same logic as Otsu--Rai for fixed-$M$ matching and Yang--Zhang for double-score matching, with the additional survey-weight factors carried through the within-match normalized coefficients and the outer H\'ajek weights.

For each bootstrap replicate $b=1,\ldots,B$, generate multinomial replication counts
\[
(m_1^{\ast,b},\ldots,m_n^{\ast,b})\sim \mathrm{Multinomial}(n; n^{-1},\ldots,n^{-1}).
\]
Under retrospective sampling define
\[
w_i^{\ast,b}:=m_i^{\ast,b}\,w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i),
\]
and under prospective sampling define
\[
w_i^{\ast,b}:=m_i^{\ast,b}\,w(\mathbf X_i).
\]
The score estimators are re-estimated with the same design-specific estimating equations used in the main text, replacing the original observation weights by the replicate weights above when the score equation is survey weighted, and replacing the unweighted score equations by their multinomial-weight analogues when the score equation is unweighted. The resulting bootstrap score estimates are inserted into the bias-corrected outcome regressions
\[
\widehat\mu_z^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X_i))
\quad\text{or}\quad
\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))
\]
as required by the cell under study.

\subsection{O.1 Retrospective PATE}

The known-score linear representation of the bias-corrected retrospective PATE estimator is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\mathrm{BC}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)}
\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATE},i}
+o_p(n^{-1/2}),
\]
where
\[
\varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATE},i}
=
 w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}
+
(2Z_i-1)\{w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)+K_{Z_i}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(i)\}\varepsilon_{Z_i,i}.
\]
The corresponding bootstrap replicate is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{R,\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_i^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n
\Big[
 w_i^{\ast,b}\{\widehat\mu_1^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X_i))
-
\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))\}
+
 m_i^{\ast,b}(2Z_i-1)\{w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i)+K_{Z_i}^{R,\mathrm{PATE}}(i)\}
 \{Y_i-\widehat\mu_{Z_i}^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i))\}
\Big].
\]

\subsection{O.2 Prospective PATE}

The known-score linear representation of the bias-corrected prospective PATE estimator is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\mathrm{BC}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^n w(\mathbf X_i)}
\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATE},i}
+o_p(n^{-1/2}),
\]
where
\[
\varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATE},i}
=
 w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}\}
+
(2Z_i-1)\{w(\mathbf X_i)+K_{Z_i}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(i)\}\varepsilon_{Z_i,i}.
\]
The bootstrap replicate is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATE}}^{P,\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^n w_i^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n
\Big[
 w_i^{\ast,b}\{\widehat\mu_1^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_1(\mathbf X_i))
-
\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))\}
+
 m_i^{\ast,b}(2Z_i-1)\{w(\mathbf X_i)+K_{Z_i}^{P,\mathrm{PATE}}(i)\}
 \{Y_i-\widehat\mu_{Z_i}^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i))\}
\Big].
\]

\subsection{O.3 Retrospective PATT}

Under the one-sided PATT route, the known-score linear representation of the bias-corrected retrospective PATT estimator is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)}
\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATT},i}
+o_p(n^{-1/2}),
\]
where
\[
\varphi_{R,\mathrm{PATT},i}
=
\begin{cases}
 w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\}, & Z_i=1,\\[1mm]
 -K_0^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(i)\varepsilon_{0,i}, & Z_i=0.
\end{cases}
\]
Equivalently, after expanding the treated-side observed outcome into mean plus residual,
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\mathrm{BC}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_1(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0} K_0^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(j)\varepsilon_{0,j}
\right]
+o_p(n^{-1/2}).
\]
The bootstrap replicate is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{R,\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}\{Y_i-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))\}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0} m_j^{\ast,b}K_0^{R,\mathrm{PATT}}(j)\{Y_j-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_j))\}
\right].
\]

\subsection{O.4 Prospective PATT}

The known-score linear representation of the bias-corrected prospective PATT estimator is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\mathrm{BC}}-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w(\mathbf X_i)}
\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATT},i}
+o_p(n^{-1/2}),
\]
where
\[
\varphi_{P,\mathrm{PATT},i}
=
\begin{cases}
 w(\mathbf X_i)\{\tau_T(\mathbf X_i)-\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}+\varepsilon_{1\mid1,i}\}, & Z_i=1,\\[1mm]
 -K_0^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(i)\varepsilon_{0,i}, & Z_i=0.
\end{cases}
\]
The bootstrap replicate is
\[
\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{PATT}}^{P,\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}}
\left[
\sum_{i:Z_i=1} w_i^{\ast,b}\{Y_i-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_i))\}
-
\sum_{j:Z_j=0} m_j^{\ast,b}K_0^{P,\mathrm{PATT}}(j)\{Y_j-\widehat\mu_0^{\mathrm{BC},\ast,b}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X_j))\}
\right].
\]

\subsection{O.5 Cell-specific bootstrap variance estimators and their large-sample targets}

For each cell $A\in\{(R,\mathrm{PATE}),(P,\mathrm{PATE}),(R,\mathrm{PATT}),(P,\mathrm{PATT})\}$, define the sample mean of the bootstrap replicates by
\[
\overline\tau_A^{\ast}
=
\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^B \widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b},
\]
and estimate the variance by
\[
\widehat{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mathrm{boot}}(\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}})
=
\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^B\bigl(\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\overline\tau_A^{\ast}\bigr)^2.
\]
Under the same known-score linearization and the same exact matching structure used in Sections~5--8, the bootstrap variance estimator targets the known-score asymptotic variance in the corresponding cell. Under the additional estimated-score local-experiment conditions of Section~10, the same replication formulas target the estimated-score asymptotic variance because the replicate score estimators obey the same first-step asymptotic linearization and the original fixed match structure preserves the matching-frequency array that standard bootstrap resampling would fail to reproduce. Consequently the fixed-law target is
\[
V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
=
V_{A,\mathrm{known\text{-}score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^{\top}\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1},
\]
while the moving-target extension is
\[
V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}
=
V_{A,\mathrm{known\text{-}score}}
-
\gamma_{A,1}^{\top}\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,1}
+
\gamma_{A,2}^{\top}\Sigma_{A,\theta}\gamma_{A,2}.
\]
The proof is the same in all four cells: substitute the corresponding asymptotic linear representation into the bootstrap replicate, center by the conditional mean given the original sample, apply the multiplier central limit theorem conditionally on the original sigma-field, and then invoke the same Slutsky and H\'ajek-normalization arguments used in the main asymptotic proofs. The only cell-specific differences are the denominator, the relevant survey-weight function, the relevant matching frequency $K_z^{\cdot}(j)$, and whether the reduced score is $(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_1(\mathbf X))$, $(e(\mathbf X),\Psi_0(\mathbf X))$, or both.


\textbf{Proposition O.1} (Cell-specific bootstrap linearization in the four WDSM settings). Fix one of the four cells
\[
A\in\{(R,\mathrm{PATE}),(P,\mathrm{PATE}),(R,\mathrm{PATT}),(P,\mathrm{PATT})\},
\]
and let
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}-\tau_A
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{A,i}+o_p(n^{-1/2})
\]
be the cell-specific asymptotic linear representation proved in the main text and summarized in Sections~5--8 and Appendix~N. Let $\omega_{A,i}$ denote the outer H\'ajek weight appearing in cell $A$:
\[
\omega_{A,i}
=
\begin{cases}
 w_{Z_i}(\mathbf X_i), & A=(R,\mathrm{PATE}),\\
 w(\mathbf X_i), & A=(P,\mathrm{PATE}),\\
 Z_iw_1(\mathbf X_i), & A=(R,\mathrm{PATT}),\\
 Z_iw(\mathbf X_i), & A=(P,\mathrm{PATT}).
\end{cases}
\]
Then the linearization-based bootstrap replicate satisfies
\[
\sqrt n\bigl(\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}\bigr)
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}
\sum_{i=1}^n (m_i^{\ast,b}-1)\bigl\{\varphi_{A,i}-\tau_A\omega_{A,i}\bigr\}
+o_{p^{\ast}}(1),
\]
where $o_{p^{\ast}}(1)$ is in conditional bootstrap probability given the original sample. Consequently, conditional on the original sample,
\[
\sqrt n\bigl(\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}\bigr)
\Rightarrow_{p}
N\!\bigl(0,V_A^{\mathrm{boot}}\bigr),
\]
with
\[
V_A^{\mathrm{boot}}=V_{A,\mathrm{known\text{-}score}}
\]
under the known-score theory, with
\[
V_A^{\mathrm{boot}}=V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}
\]
under the fixed-law estimated-score theory, and with
\[
V_A^{\mathrm{boot}}=V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}
\]
under the moving-target estimated-score extension.

\textbf{Proof.} We write the H\'ajek expansion one algebraic step at a time. Let
\[
\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}:=\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\omega_{A,i},
\qquad
\widehat N_A:=\sum_{i=1}^n \omega_{A,i}.
\]
By construction of the bootstrap replicate in the relevant cell,
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}^{\ast,b},
\]
where $\varphi_{A,i}^{\ast,b}$ is the bootstrap version of the same linear term. Add and subtract the unperturbed linear term inside the numerator:
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}
-
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{A,i}
+
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}(\varphi_{A,i}^{\ast,b}-\varphi_{A,i}).
\]
Under the same first-step replication rate conditions used in Section~10 and Section~10.1B, the last term is $o_{p^{\ast}}(n^{-1/2})$. Therefore
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}}
\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}
-
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{A,i}
+
 o_{p^{\ast}}(n^{-1/2}).
\]
Now add and subtract $\widehat N_A^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}$:
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}
=
\left(\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}}-\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}\right)
\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}
+
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}
\sum_{i=1}^n (m_i^{\ast,b}-1)\varphi_{A,i}
+
 o_{p^{\ast}}(n^{-1/2}).
\]
Next linearize the inverse denominator:
\[
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}}-\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}
=
-\frac{\widehat N_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat N_A}{\widehat N_A^2}
+o_{p^{\ast}}(n^{-1})
=
-\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^2}
\sum_{i=1}^n (m_i^{\ast,b}-1)\omega_{A,i}
+o_{p^{\ast}}(n^{-1}).
\]
Substitute this into the previous display:
\[
\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}
=
-\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^2}
\left\{\sum_{i=1}^n (m_i^{\ast,b}-1)\omega_{A,i}\right\}
\left\{\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}\right\}
+
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}
\sum_{i=1}^n (m_i^{\ast,b}-1)\varphi_{A,i}
+
 o_{p^{\ast}}(n^{-1/2}).
\]
Because $\widehat N_A^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n \varphi_{A,i}=\tau_A+o_p(1)$ and $n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n m_i^{\ast,b}\varphi_{A,i}=O_{p^{\ast}}(1)$, the product term simplifies to the usual H\'ajek centering correction. Multiply by $\sqrt n$:
\[
\sqrt n\bigl(\widehat\tau_A^{\ast,b}-\widehat\tau_A^{\mathrm{BC}}\bigr)
=
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A}
\frac{1}{\sqrt n}
\sum_{i=1}^n (m_i^{\ast,b}-1)\bigl\{\varphi_{A,i}-\tau_A\omega_{A,i}\bigr\}
+
 o_{p^{\ast}}(1).
\]
This is the displayed bootstrap linearization. The conditional multiplier central limit theorem now yields the Gaussian limit with variance equal to the conditional limit of
\[
\frac{1}{\widehat N_A^2}
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{i=1}^n \bigl\{\varphi_{A,i}-\tau_A\omega_{A,i}\bigr\}^2.
\]
Under the known-score theory this conditional variance converges to $V_{A,\mathrm{known\text{-}score}}$. Under the fixed-law estimated-score theory the same argument applies to the score-adjusted linear representation and yields $V_{A,\mathrm{fix}}$. Under the moving-target extension the same argument applies to the moving-target linear representation and yields $V_{A,\mathrm{chg}}$. The bootstrap variance estimator therefore converges to the same large-sample variance target as the corresponding point estimator. \(\square\)




\section{Appendix P. Optional equal-share validation route and residual-variance closure conditions on the score space}

\textbf{Why Appendix~P is separate from the main exact-share theorem path.} The results collected below are optional validation devices used only when one wants to collapse the exact survey-weight-normalized within-match coefficients to the equal-share benchmark formulas. They are not used in the general exact-share theorem path in Sections~5--8.

\textbf{Corollary 4.8A} (Local matched unit-weight homogeneity as an optional
sufficient collapse to the equal-share formulas). For each focal unit
\(i\in\mathcal I_R\), define the matched-set average survey weight of the matched unit
\[\bar b_{D,n}(i)
\coloneqq
\frac1M\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)} w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_k),\] and the matched-set
matched unit-survey-weight oscillation \[\Delta_{n,D}(i)
\coloneqq
\max_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)}\bigl|w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_k)-\bar b_{D,n}(i)\bigr|.\]
Assume there exist constants \(0<\underline w_D\le \bar w_D<\infty\)
such that \[\underline w_D\le w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X)\le \bar w_D
\qquad\text{almost surely on the matched arm},\] and suppose
\[E_s\bigl[\Delta_{n,D}(i)^2\mid i\in\mathcal I_R\bigr]\to 0,
\qquad
E_s\bigl[\Delta_{n,D}(i)^4\mid i\in\mathcal I_R\bigr]\to 0.\] Then the
exact matched unit-share factors satisfy \[s_{D,1}(d)=\frac1M,
\qquad
s_{D,2}(d)=\frac1{M^2},
\qquad
s_{D,11}(d)=\frac1{M^2}\] for \(f_D^s\)-almost every \(d\). Consequently
Theorem 4.8 reduces to
\[\kappa_{D,1}^{\mathrm{ex}}(d)=\frac{f_{R,D}^{(1)}(d)}{f_D^s(d)},\]
\[\kappa_{D,2}^{\mathrm{ex}}(d)=\frac1M\frac{f_{R,D}^{(2)}(d)}{f_D^s(d)}+
\frac{\alpha(M,2)}{M^2}\left\{\frac{f_{R,D}^{(1)}(d)}{f_D^s(d)}\right\}^2.\]

\textbf{Proof.} For any focal unit \(i\in\mathcal I_R\) and matched unit
\(j\in\mathcal J_D(i)\), \[\begin{aligned}
\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}
&=
\frac{w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_j)}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)} w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_k)}
=
\frac{w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_j)}{M\bar b_{D,n}(i)}\\
&=
\frac{\bar b_{D,n}(i)}{M\bar b_{D,n}(i)}+
\frac{w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_j)-\bar b_{D,n}(i)}{M\bar b_{D,n}(i)}\\
&=
\frac1M+
\frac{w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_j)-\bar b_{D,n}(i)}{M\bar b_{D,n}(i)}.
\end{aligned}\] Therefore
\[\left|\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}-\frac1M\right|
\le
\frac{\Delta_{n,D}(i)}{M\underline w_D}.\] Take squares:
\[\left|\left(\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}\right)^2-\frac1{M^2}\right|
\le
\left|\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}-\frac1M\right|
\left(\left|\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}\right|+\frac1M\right)
\le
C\,\Delta_{n,D}(i)\] for some deterministic constant \(C<\infty\),
because \(w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X)\) is bounded above and below. The product case is
similar: \[\begin{aligned}
\left|\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}\Lambda_{\ell\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}-\frac1{M^2}\right|
&\le
\left|\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}-\frac1M\right|\left|\Lambda_{\ell\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}\right|
+
\frac1M\left|\Lambda_{\ell\to j,n}^{(D),\mathrm{ex}}-\frac1M\right|\\
&\le
C\{\Delta_{n,D}(i)+\Delta_{n,D}(\ell)\}
\end{aligned}\] for a deterministic constant \(C<\infty\). The stated
second and fourth moment assumptions on \(\Delta_{n,D}(i)\) then force
the three ratio limits in Closure Input 4.7A to take the equal-share values.
\(\square\)



\textbf{Lemma 4.8B} (Optional score-smooth matched unit-weight route to
Corollary 4.8A). Fix a matched-arm score space \(D\) and suppose that on the
matched arm the matched-arm survey weight admits a score representation
\[
w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X)=b_D(\mathbf D(\mathbf X))
\]
for some function \(b_D\) satisfying
\[
0<\underline w_D\le b_D(d)\le \bar w_D<\infty
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
|b_D(d)-b_D(d')|\le L_D\|d-d'\|.
\]
Let
\[
\rho_{n,D}(i):=\max_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)}\|D(\mathbf X_k)-D(\mathbf X_i)\|.
\]
Then for every focal unit \(i\),
\[
\Delta_{n,D}(i)\le 2L_D\,\rho_{n,D}(i).
\]
Consequently,
\[
E_s[\Delta_{n,D}(i)^2\mid i\in\mathcal I_R]=O(n^{-1})\to 0,
\]
and, because \(\Delta_{n,D}(i)\le 2\bar w_D\),
\[
E_s[\Delta_{n,D}(i)^4\mid i\in\mathcal I_R]\to 0.
\]
Hence the moment conditions required in Corollary 4.8A follow from the
matched unit-weight score smoothness and the already proved two-dimensional
score-radius rate.

\textbf{Proof.} Fix a focal unit \(i\). Write \(d_i=D(\mathbf X_i)\) and
\(d_k=D(\mathbf X_k)\) for \(k\in\mathcal J_D(i)\). By definition,
\[
\bar b_{D,n}(i)=\frac1M\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)} b_D(d_k).
\]
Subtract and add \(b_D(d_i)\):
\[
\begin{aligned}
|\bar b_{D,n}(i)-b_D(d_i)|
&=
\left|
\frac1M\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)}\{b_D(d_k)-b_D(d_i)\}
\right|\\
&\le
\frac1M\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)}|b_D(d_k)-b_D(d_i)|\\
&\le
\frac{L_D}{M}\sum_{k\in\mathcal J_D(i)}\|d_k-d_i\|\\
&\le
L_D\,\rho_{n,D}(i).
\end{aligned}
\]
Now fix any matched unit \(j\in\mathcal J_D(i)\). Then
\[
\begin{aligned}
|w_D^{(D)}(\mathbf X_j)-\bar b_{D,n}(i)|
&=
|b_D(d_j)-\bar b_{D,n}(i)|\\
&\le
|b_D(d_j)-b_D(d_i)|+|b_D(d_i)-\bar b_{D,n}(i)|\\
&\le
L_D\|d_j-d_i\|+L_D\rho_{n,D}(i)\\
&\le
2L_D\rho_{n,D}(i).
\end{aligned}
\]
Take the maximum over \(j\in\mathcal J_D(i)\) to obtain the first
display. Squaring and taking expectations,
\[
E_s[\Delta_{n,D}(i)^2\mid i\in\mathcal I_R]
\le
4L_D^2 E_s[\rho_{n,D}(i)^2\mid i\in\mathcal I_R]
=O(n^{-1})
\]
by Lemma~4.3. Because \(0\le \Delta_{n,D}(i)\le 2\bar w_D\),
\[
\Delta_{n,D}(i)^4\le (2\bar w_D)^2\Delta_{n,D}(i)^2.
\]
Take expectations to obtain the fourth-moment claim. The final sentence
now follows from Corollary~4.8A. \(\square\)

\textbf{Remark 4.8B.} Lemma 4.8B is deliberately optional. It is used
only if one wants to collapse the exact-share formulas to the
equal-share formulas. The main exact theorem path does not require the
survey weight of the matched unit to be a smooth function of the matching score.


\subsubsection{4.4.6 Optional equal-share subclass implying the cell-specific residual stabilization blocks}\label{primitive-sufficient-route-to-the-cell-specific-residual-stabilization-blocks}

\textbf{Imported Input 4.12B} (Equal-share coefficient-square stabilization on a
two-dimensional matching-score space). Fix a matched-arm score space
\(\mathbf D\in\mathbb R^2\) and a fixed number of matches \(M\). For the
equal-share coefficient array built on the same nearest-neighbor graph,
the fixed-\(M\), two-dimensional matching literature gives a variance
closure on the matching-score scale: once the matched-arm score density
is continuous and bounded away from zero, the relevant mark functions are
continuous and bounded, and the matching rule is the ordinary Euclidean
\(M\)-nearest-neighbor rule, the sample average of the equal-share
coefficient squares converges in probability to the corresponding
matching-score integral with the Chen--Han geometric constant
\(\alpha(M,2)\). This is the score-space version of the fixed-\(M\)
variance closure proved through the Abadie--Imbens martingale route and
the Chen--Han two-dimensional catchment-geometry route.

\textbf{Proposition 4.12C} (Optional sufficient closure after the local equal-share collapse). Fix any one of the four cells. Assume:
\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item the local oscillation condition in Corollary~4.8A holds on the relevant matched-arm score space, so the exact within-match
survey-weight-normalized share collapses to the equal-share value \(1/M\);
\item the matched-arm score density and the cell-specific mark functions
appearing in the residual quadratic variation are continuous and bounded
on the corresponding two-dimensional matching-score space;
\item Imported Input~4.12B applies on that score space.
\end{enumerate}
Then the corresponding cell-specific residual stabilization block in
Sections~5--8 holds automatically. In other words, under this optional
equal-share validation route the centered known-score theorem does not
require an additional cell-specific stabilization input.

\textbf{Proof.} Fix a cell and write its residual coefficient generically
as
\[
\kappa_{n,A}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)
=
\kappa_{n,A}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)+r_{n,A}(j),
\]
where \(\kappa_{n,A}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)\) is the equal-share coefficient
obtained by replacing the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
share \(\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(\mathbf D),\mathrm{ex}}\) with \(1/M\), and
\(r_{n,A}(j)\) is the remainder. Under Corollary~4.8A,
the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized share satisfies
\[
\Lambda_{i\to j,n}^{(\mathbf D),\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac1M+o_p(1)
\]
on the local matched set, with the remainder controlled by the matched-arm
score radius through the oscillation bound displayed in the proof of
Corollary~4.8A. Hence, after multiplying by any bounded continuous
cell-specific residual-variance mark,
\[
\frac1n\sum_j
\left\{
\kappa_{n,A}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)^2
-
\kappa_{n,A}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)^2
\right\}
=
o_p(1).
\]

Now apply Imported Input~4.12B to the equal-share coefficient array on
the same two-dimensional matching-score space. Because the matching graph
itself is unchanged, only the coefficient algebra changes, and that
coefficient algebra has already been collapsed to the equal-share form by
Corollary~4.8A, the sample quadratic variation built from
\(\kappa_{n,A}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)^2\) converges in probability to the
corresponding matching-score integral with the cell-specific first and
second marked densities and the same Chen--Han geometric constant
\(\alpha(M,2)\). Combining the equal-share stabilization limit with the
\(o_p(1)\) remainder displayed above yields the same convergence for the
exact coefficient square. The Lyapunov part follows in the same way,
because the arm-specific residuals have bounded \((2+\delta)\)-moments
and the equal-share coefficient array has the same fixed-\(M\),
two-dimensional stabilization order. This proves the claim. \(\square\)



\subsection{P.1 Score-indexed residual-variance functions}

For retrospective and prospective PATE, define
\[
\nu_{A,z}(d)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-m_{s,z}(d)\mid \mathbf D_z(\mathbf X)=d,\ Z=z
\right),
\qquad
z\in\{0,1\},
\]
for \(A\in\{R\text{-PATE},P\text{-PATE}\}\). For retrospective and
prospective PATT, define the treated-arm and control-arm variance
objects by
\[
\nu_{A,1}^{T}(\mathbf X)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-\mu_{1\mid 1}(\mathbf X)\mid \mathbf X,\ Z=1
\right),
\]
\[
\nu_{A,0}^{T}(d)
:=
\operatorname{Var}_s\!\left(
Y-m_{s,0}(d)\mid \mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)=d,\ Z=0
\right),
\]
for \(A\in\{R\text{-PATT},P\text{-PATT}\}\).

\textbf{Remark P.1.} In the exact conditional-variance lemmas of
Sections~5--8 the conditional variance naturally appears as
\(\sigma_z^2(\mathbf X_j)\) or \(\sigma_{1\mid1}^2(\mathbf X_j)\)
because one is conditioning on the full covariate sigma-field. The
probability limits, however, live on the matching-score space. The
functions \(\nu_{A,z}(d)\) and \(\nu_{A,0}^{T}(d)\) are exactly the
score-indexed residual-variance objects that appear in the final
score-space formulas.

\subsection{P.2 Imported equal-share coefficient-square limits}

Fix one cell \(A\). Under Corollary~4.8A and Lemma~4.8B, the exact
within-match survey-weight-normalized coefficient reduces locally to the
equal-share coefficient \(1/M\). Let
\[
\chi_{A,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(d)
\]
denote the corresponding fixed-\(M\), two-dimensional coefficient-square
probability limit on the relevant score space for arm \(z\), imported
from the published unweighted geometry of Abadie--Imbens (2006, detailed
proof version) and Chen--Han (2024). In the PATT cells the treated
self-term does not involve matching reuse; its coefficient-square limit
is simply \(w_1(\mathbf X)^2\) in the retrospective cell and
\(w(\mathbf X)^2\) in the prospective cell.

\subsection{P.3 Primitive sufficient theorem for Closure Inputs~5.4, 6.4, 7.5, and~8.5}

\textbf{Theorem P.2} (Primitive sufficient route to the residual
quadratic-variation limits). Fix one of the four cells
\[
A\in\{R\text{-PATE},R\text{-PATT},P\text{-PATE},P\text{-PATT}\}.
\]
Assume:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  the primitive design and score-space regularity assumptions for that
  cell in Sections~2--4;
\item
  the local matched-unit-weight homogeneity route of Corollary~4.8A and
  Lemma~4.8B on the relevant score space;
\item
  the imported fixed-\(M\), two-dimensional equal-share
  coefficient-square stabilization theorem on the same score space;
\item
  bounded continuity of the relevant score-indexed residual-variance
  functions from Section~P.1 and bounded fourth moments of the residuals.
\end{enumerate}

Then the corresponding closure condition among
Closure Inputs~5.4, 6.4, 7.5, and~8.5 holds. Moreover the
large-sample residual-variance target is written on the score space as
follows.

\textbf{Retrospective PATE.}
\[
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{\{E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)]\}^2}
\sum_{z=0}^{1}
P_s(Z=z)
E_s\!\left[
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
\,
\chi_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
\;\middle|\; Z=z
\right].
\]

\textbf{Prospective PATE.}
\[
V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATE}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\frac{1}{\{E_s[w(\mathbf X)]\}^2}
\sum_{z=0}^{1}
P_s(Z=z)
E_s\!\left[
\nu_{P,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
\,
\chi_{P,\mathrm{PATE},z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
\;\middle|\; Z=z
\right].
\]

\textbf{Retrospective PATT.}
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{E,R,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
&=
\frac{1}{\{E_s[Zw_1(\mathbf X)]\}^2}
\Biggl[
P_s(Z=1)
E_s\!\left\{
  w_1(\mathbf X)^2
  \nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)
  \mid Z=1
\right\}
\\
&\qquad\qquad
+
P_s(Z=0)
E_s\!\left\{
  \nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{T}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
  \,
  \chi_{R,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
  \mid Z=0
\right\}
\Biggr].
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{Prospective PATT.}
\[
\begin{aligned}
V_{E,P,\mathrm{PATT}}^{\mathrm{ex}}
&=
\frac{1}{\{E_s[Zw(\mathbf X)]\}^2}
\Biggl[
P_s(Z=1)
E_s\!\left\{
  w(\mathbf X)^2
  \nu_{P,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)
  \mid Z=1
\right\}
\\
&\qquad\qquad
+
P_s(Z=0)
E_s\!\left\{
  \nu_{P,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{T}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
  \,
  \chi_{P,\mathrm{PATT},0}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X))
  \mid Z=0
\right\}
\Biggr].
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} We write the proof for retrospective PATE; the other
three cells are the same coefficient argument with the cell-specific
weights and one-sided score substitutions.

For each arm \(z\in\{0,1\}\), let
\[
c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)
:=
w_z(\mathbf X_j)+K_{n,z}^{R,\mathrm{PATE},\mathrm{ex}}(j),
\]
and let \(c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)\) denote the corresponding
equal-share coefficient obtained by replacing the exact within-match
survey-weight-normalized share by \(1/M\). By Corollary~4.8A and
Lemma~4.8B, the exact share and the equal share differ by a locally
vanishing amount on each matched set. Therefore the induced armwise
coefficients satisfy, after summing over focal units and using bounded
weights,
\[
\frac1n\sum_{j:Z_j=z}
\left|
c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)-c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)
\right|^2
\to_p 0.
\]
Expand the difference of squares one object at a time:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\frac1n\sum_{j:Z_j=z}
\left|
\{c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)\}^2-\{c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)\}^2
\right|
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X_j))\\
&=
\frac1n\sum_{j:Z_j=z}
\left|
c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)-c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)
\right|
\left|
c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)+c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)
\right|
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X_j)).
\end{aligned}
\]
Because the coefficients are uniformly bounded under the present
assumptions and the score-indexed residual variances are bounded, the
last display is \(o_p(1)\). Hence the exact coefficient-square average
and the equal-share coefficient-square average have the same probability
limit.

Now invoke the imported two-dimensional equal-share coefficient-square
stabilization theorem on the score space. For each arm \(z\),
\[
\frac1n\sum_{j:Z_j=z}
\{c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)\}^2
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X_j))
\to_p
P_s(Z=z)
E_s\!\left[
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
\,
\chi_{R,\mathrm{PATE},z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf D_z(\mathbf X))
\mid Z=z
\right].
\]
By the previous reduction, the same limit holds with
\(c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{ex}}(j)\) in place of \(c_{n,z}^{\mathrm{eq}}(j)\).
Substitute the two armwise limits into the predictable quadratic
variation formula from Lemma~5.3 and use the Hájek denominator law of
large numbers
\[
\bar m_{R,\mathrm{PATE},n}\to_p E_s[w_Z(\mathbf X)].
\]
This yields the displayed retrospective PATE limit.

For retrospective PATT and prospective PATT, the treated self term
contains no matching reuse, so its contribution is just the Hájek limit
of the treated self coefficient square,
\[
\frac1n\sum_{j:Z_j=1} w_1(\mathbf X_j)^2
\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X_j)
\to_p
P_s(Z=1)
E_s\!\left\{
w_1(\mathbf X)^2\nu_{R,\mathrm{PATT},1}^{T}(\mathbf X)\mid Z=1
\right\},
\]
or the prospective analogue with \(w(\mathbf X)^2\). The control arm is
handled by the same exact-to-equal-share reduction and the same imported
two-dimensional coefficient-square stabilization theorem on the
one-sided score space \(\mathbf D_0(\mathbf X)\). Therefore the
corresponding closure condition holds in each of the four cells.
\(\square\)

\textbf{Remark P.2.} Appendix~P does not eliminate the exact-share main
theorem path. It provides a primitive sufficient route in the important
smooth-weight subclass. The exact-share theorem body remains valuable
because it keeps the exact within-match survey-weight-normalized
coefficient visible and does not silently replace it by \(1/M\). The
present appendix simply ensures that the publication-level objection to
leaving Closure Inputs~5.4, 6.4, 7.5, and~8.5 as bare shortcuts is
answered in a mathematically explicit subclass.
